ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u38	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present to the High House a report on the implementation of the state budget in 2016, prints 1588 and 1721. First of all, I would like to recall the words of the chairman of the Committee on Public Finances, then Mr Andrzej Jaworski, who in his speech in plenary, where the second reading of the draft budget law for 2016 took place, recalled that the macroeconomic assumptions underlying the draft law were prepared on the basis of national and international data available in 2015. He added that these assumptions did not differ from those adopted by the previous government when he drew up a draft budget law for 2016. President Jaworski was aware that over time, and especially in the circumstances of international developments, these assumptions would be reviewed, which could mean that in practice not all variables would evolve as expected. At the same time, it is worth recalling that these projections were not optimistic, as experts pointed out in early 2016 that the Polish economy would continue to operate in a difficult external environment, which meant that it should, like other European economies, further prepare for the absorption of the effects of the anticipated dangerous scenario of the third phase of the crisis resulting from the risky debts of companies from countries such as China, Brazil, Turkey and Russia. From today's perspective, we can see that the 2016 budget was implemented in relatively favourable macroeconomic conditions, because the dangerous scenarios of international developments were not fulfilled, and also the positive effects of the government's economic and social policy began to emerge. In 2016, GDP growth at stable prices was 2.7% and was lower than assumed by 1.1 percentage points, but at the same time was 0.8 percentage points higher than in the European Union, where it was 1.9%. The external balance was maintained, there was an excess of exports over imports. The weakening of the rate of gold compared to previous years was conducive to an increase in exports, but on the other hand it did not serve to reduce the costs of handling the Treasury's debt. The current account deficit in the balance of payments developed at a low level and increased the value of foreign exchange reserves. The situation on the labour market was improving, as unemployment and employment were gradually falling. Only year-to-year deflation that persists over 10 months has not been conducive to a greater increase in budgetary revenue. Compared to the assumptions made, the investment that both governments, the government that prepared the draft budget - the government of the Civic Platform and the PSL - and the government of Law and Justice that took over the budget - were planning for 7% and the European Commission was planning for 5.5%. Instead, there was a decrease in investments of 7,9%. In this respect, the assumptions were overly optimistic, among others. underestimating the effect of changing the EU's financial perspective. I will now discuss the general situation of the budget in 2016. When assessing the overall situation of Polish public finances in 2016, it should be noted first of all that the deficit of the public finance sector calculated according to the national methodology increased from 2,4% GDP in 2015 to 2,5% GDP in 2016, i.e. slightly, given the lower than expected economic growth. During the year, the general government deficit was maintained below the 3% Treaty limit. GDP. This phenomenon is worth emphasising that the sector's deficit, which ultimately represents 2.4% GDP has taken place in the context of costly changes in social policy, including in particular the 500+ family programme. At the same time, public debt calculated according to the EU methodology did not exceed 60% GDP, albeit increased from 51.1% up to 54.4% GDP, mainly due to changes in exchange rates. To sum up, Poland in 2016 met the fiscal criteria applicable in Economic and Monetary Union, which should be considered a beneficial phenomenon. On the other hand, Poland's public finances still have weaknesses such as in previous years, and therefore the process of public finance reform should be continued consistently. Action is still needed to consolidate and improve the structure of Polish public finances. When assessing the implementation of the revenue and expenditure of the budget in 2016, it can be concluded that the revenue realised amounted to almost 314.7% billion PLN and they were higher than the original budget assumptions by PLN 875 million, i.e. 0.3%, and compared to 2015, it was higher by PLN 25.5 billion, i.e. by 8.8%. There was a change in the structure of income. The share of non-tax income without income from the European Union increased by 3.2 percentage points, while the share of tax revenue decreased by 3 percentage points. The increase in the share of non-tax revenues in total income was decided by the payment from the profit of the National Bank of Poland in 2016. - this was not the case in 2015. - and high one-off revenues recorded in 2016 for the so-called frequency reservation. the digital dividend, i.e. approx. PLN 9.2 billion. In 2016, revenue from value added tax amounted to PLN 126.6 billion and was slightly lower, by PLN 2.1 billion, from the amount forecasted in the Budget Act. This means that the implementation of the forecast of VAT revenue contained in the Budget Act was implemented at 98.4%. However, compared to 2015, VAT revenues were 2,8% higher. One of the reasons for the non-performance of VAT revenue forecast in the 2016 Budget Act and the relatively low increase in VAT revenue compared to previous years were the increased payment of systemic reimbursements to businesses in December 2016. In such a situation, in order to properly assess VAT receipts in 2016, the rate of change in tax revenue should be compared with the rate of change in the macroeconomic VAT base, the comparison of VAT receipts on an accrual basis applied by Eurostat, which is comparable to the methodology used for assessing national accounts. In 2016, VAT revenues on an accrual basis of ESA 2010 amounted to PLN 131 billion and were higher by PLN 5.2 billion, i.e. 4.1% , in relation to revenue in 2015 At the same time, in 2016 we had a significant improvement in the so-called. tax collection. This effect can be estimated at about PLN 3 billion in 2016. A number of measures taken by the Ministry of Finance to improve the efficiency of VAT collection, including legislative measures concerning, inter alia, the improvement of tax collection. the modification of the conditions of operation of the reverse charge mechanism and the extension of its scope to include transactions involving electronics products, the introduction of an obligation to report monthly records and the entry into force of the so-called. the fuel and energy package. In 2016, excise revenue amounted to PLN 65,7 billion and amounted to 102,6% forecasts from the budget law. Compared to 2015, excise revenue increased by PLN 2.9 billion, i.e. 4.7%. The increase in revenue as compared to planned and for 2015 has been noted in all major items, including energy products, tobacco and alcohol and passenger cars, and has resulted, among others. from the legal changes introduced in 2016, the actions of the control services aimed at combating the grey zone and the increased demand for consumption. In 2016, higher revenues from gambling tax and income taxes were achieved than planned in the Budget Act and higher revenues from gaming tax and income taxes than in 2015. And so the proceeds from the gaming tax amounted to PLN 1.4 billion, which was 101.7% the forecasts from the Budget Act, while compared to the 2015 implementation, these revenues were 5.2% higher. The revenues from personal income tax were realised at the amount of PLN 48,2 billion and were higher than planned by 2,9%. , and by 7.1% compared to 2015. At the same time, corporate income tax revenues were realised in the amount of PLN 26.4 billion, which was 101.2% plan for 2016 and 102,2% revenue realised in 2015. In 2016 it was not possible to achieve in the planned amount of tax on the extraction of certain fossils, tax on certain financial institutions and tax on large-area shops - the implementation was lower than the plan by a total amount of PLN 4.2 billion. At the same time, this loss was compensated for by non-tax revenues above planned, mainly from a higher contribution from the NBP's profit and from revenue from state budget units and other non-tax revenues. In the budget law, the expenditure of the state budget is planned at PLN 368.5 billion. This limit has not changed despite the double amendment of the Budget Act. State budget expenditure amounted to PLN 360.8 billion in 2016, of which PLN 750 million accounted for expenditure shifted to implementation in 2017. The expenditure implemented was lower by PLN 7.7 billion compared to the amount set out in the 2016 Budget Act, which represented 2.1% expenditure planned. Among the expenditures that have been made to the lowest extent compared to the post-change plan, there are funds allocated to co-financing projects involving European Union funds - PLN 3.3 billion, current expenditure of budgetary units - PLN 1.4 billion and property expenditure - PLN 0.6 billion. Most of the unused funds - approx. PLN 3 billion - were included in the special provisions for co-financing projects implemented with European funds, which resulted mainly from savings resulting from the tender procedures carried out, the transfer of project implementation to the following financial year and the savings under the funds planned directly in the budgetary parts of the individual authorising officers. Compared to 2015, the expenditure of the state budget in 2016 was higher by PLN 29.1 billion, i.e. 8.8%. The implementation of the expenditure was ensured, among others.: financing the government's priority task in the field of family policy, i.e. The 500+ family programme; the introduction of free medicines for people aged 75 or over; the increase in wages for workers groups which since 2010 have, in principle, been covered by the so-called 'free medicines'. freezing wages; payment of one-off cash allowances to certain pensioners and pensioners; financing of restructuring tasks in the hard coal mining sector and raising national defence expenditure to 2% GDP. The deficit of the state budget amounted to PLN 46.2 billion. It turned out to be lower by PLN 8.6 billion, i.e. 15.7% , in relation to the planned. The implementation of the basic size of the budget indicates that the 2016 budget implementation report deserves to be adopted. In quite favourable economic conditions, despite problems with the implementation of some of the assumed revenue, the expenditure savings have led to a budget deficit below the level laid down in the Budget Act. In presenting other aspects of budget implementation, I would like to say that Poland received PLN 42.9 billion from the European Union budget in 2016 and paid PLN 19.2 billion to the European Union budget. This means that the Polish economy gained net funds of PLN 23.8 billion. The expenditure of the budget of European funds amounted to PLN 51.7 billion in 2016. They accounted for 72.2% The plan. Compared to the implementation in 2015, they were lower by 23.6%. This was due to the end of the EU's financial perspective 2007 The implementation of selected strategic objectives, defined among others: in the long-term development strategy of the country. Poland 2030 and the country's development strategy 2020, which is correlated with the European programming document.Europe 2020, was satisfactory, albeit in a diverse way, in 2016. Also, monitoring of all objectives was not controlled everywhere due to the envisaged adoption of a new strategy, i.e. A strategy for responsible development by 2020 (with a view to 2030) called the Moravian Plan. In 2016 the budget law set a limit on guarantees and guarantees of PLN 200 billion, i.e. the same amount as in 2015. This limit was used in 8.3%, which should be assessed positively in view of the preventive nature of the above measures, namely to safeguard the potential use of the Treasury guarantee in order to support those at risk of the possible consequences of the financial and economic crisis. The biggest guarantees and guarantees were granted for the liabilities of Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego - PLN 88 billion, including the support of the National Road Fund - PLN 86.4 billion, which represented 70.6% the total amount of potential non-required liabilities of the State Treasury at the end of 2016 The amount of potential liabilities arising from the loans and issued bonds of Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego was fully classified into commitments with a low risk of repayment from budgetary resources. The Treasury debt at the end of 2016 amounted to PLN 928.7 billion, i.e. increased by PLN 94.1 billion in the course of the year. The main reason for the increase in this debt was the financing of the government budget deficit and the deficit of the European budget of PLN 58.8 billion. Moreover, due to exchange rate differences, the Treasury's debt increased by another PLN 14.9 billion. The share of national debt in total debt was 65.6% , and foreign debt - 34,4%. Compared to the end of 2015, these shares have changed by 0.5 percentage points in favour of domestic debt, which should be assessed positively. The net lending needs of the state budget amounted to PLN 57.1 billion last year and were PLN 26.5 billion lower than planned. The financing of loan needs was almost entirely obtained by borrowing debt on the domestic market. That was PLN 56.9 billion. The funds obtained on the foreign market were only in a small amount, PLN 200 million, intended to finance needs occurring in 2016. The Minister of Finance also used gold and currency funds from the consolidation of the funds of the public finance and monetary consolidation sector, collected in the accounts at the National Bank of Poland and Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego. The negative aspect of budget implementation was that in 2016 there was a high level of arrears towards the state budget, PLN 96,9 billion, but it is nevertheless worth noting that this was partly the result of an increased frequency of detection of irregularities during the audit. In 2016, the average employment in the national economy amounted to 10 million 45 thousand. people and was 2.3% higher compared to the previous year. Average employment in state budget units amounted to around 501.5 thousand. people and was higher by 0.1% than in 2015. The employment rate in the 15/64 age group, calculated by the BAEL method, was the highest since the 1990s in 2016. In 2015 62.9% were employed people from this age group and in 2016 - 64.5% already. It also increased to 46.2% the employment rate of persons aged 55 and 64 years. In 2004, the value of this indicator was only 28%. The progress made since then in terms of professional activation of older people should be considered to be very significant. Economic growth in Poland was possible not only because of the increased use of human resources, but also because of the improvement of labour productivity. According to Eurostat estimates in 2016, labour productivity in Poland increased by 2% on an hourly basis compared to the previous year, while on average in the European Union this increase was 0.6%. The number of unemployed registered in employment offices at the end of 2016 was around 1.3 million, i.e. It was about 228,000 lower. than at the end of 2015. The unemployment rate at the end of 2016 was 8.3%, which means that it decreased by 1.4 percentage points compared to the previous year. 2016 was the second year in a row in which wages increased dynamically. Average monthly gross remuneration in the national economy increased by 4.2% in 2016, after the inflation effects were eliminated. , and in total in the years 2015 and 2016 - by 8,9%. It is worth noting that in 2016, as in 2014 and 2015 the salaries of employees in the business sector increased much faster than the salaries of employees in the budgetary sphere. Over the last three years, the real gross average wage in the enterprise sector has increased by 13.1% in total. , while the salaries of the staff in the budgetary sphere have increased by an average of 10.5%. The increase in wage levels in 2016 was influenced not only by the improvement in the labour market situation, but also by the increase from 1 January 2016 in the minimum wage to 1850 PLN. The average monthly pension payment from the Social Insurance Fund amounted to PLN 2004 and was higher by 1.9%. in relation to the previous year. The average pension benefit financed by the KRUS Pension Fund amounted to PLN 1183 in 2016 and was higher by 0,3% compared to the previous year. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, In carrying out the obligation under Article. 204 of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, I have the honour to present to the High House an analysis of the implementation of the state budget and monetary policy assumptions in 2016 together with the opinion of the College of the Supreme Audit Chamber on discharge for the Council of Ministers for 2016. The Supreme Audit Chamber, following a review of the implementation of the State budget and an analysis of its findings, assessed positively the implementation of the 2016 budget law. The Supreme Audit Chamber also concluded that the report on the implementation of the State budget for the period from 1 January to 31 December 2016 drawn up by the Council of Ministers provides, in all material respects, reliable information and data on the amount of revenue, expenditure, debts and liabilities and the result of the State budget and the budget of European funds. The data shown in this report are reliable in the assessment of the NIK, as they result from the reports of the individual State budget managers. These evaluations were drawn up on the basis of the results of 327 audits in 307 budgetary units. In total, the implementation of the budget by the managers of 111 parts of the state budget and the financial plans of 33 out-of-budget economy units were audited. The use of grants from 104 selected beneficiaries has also been checked. The detailed results of the audit are presented in separate information provided to the Marshal and to the Sejm committees. This information was dominated by positive assessments. In one case, the Polish Space Agency, the Supreme Audit Chamber has assessed the implementation of the financial plan negatively. When the criteria for issuing a positive assessment were not met and the scale of the irregularity did not justify a negative assessment, we used descriptive assessments. They represented around 20% of total assessments. The Supreme Audit Chamber has given a positive opinion on the correctness of the accounts for all the 20 budgetary parts audited in this respect. A positive opinion was also given to 95% the annual budgetary reports examined in 104 budgetary parts and 189 third-degree directors. The state budget and the budget of European funds, as well as the financial plans of non-budget units in the public finance sector, have been implemented in accordance with the budgetary law, although they have been implemented under less favourable macroeconomic conditions than a year before, less favourable than projected. The growth rate of gross domestic product in the world economy was 3% versus 3.2% in 2015. Gross domestic product in 2016 in the European Union increased by 1,9% , while the year before this increase was 0.3 percentage points higher. Gross domestic product in Poland in 2016 increased by 2,7% in real terms. This increase was 1.1 percentage points lower than a year ago. The main driver of economic development was domestic demand. Private consumption has helped to improve income at the disposal of households as a result of good labour market conditions and the implementation of the 500+ family programme. The decrease in gross domestic demand was due to a decrease in investment. Investment expenditure in 2016 decreased by almost 8% in relation to expenditure in the previous year. The reduction in the scale of investments concerned almost all Eastern European countries belonging to the European Union. This was due to a decrease in the dynamics of the implementation of investment projects co-financed by the European Union budget at the turn of the financial perspectives for the period 2007 to 2013 and 2014 to 2020. In 2016, for example, investments in Hungary decreased by 15%. , in Slovakia - by 9 and in the Czech Republic - by 3.7%. The unemployment rate recorded in Poland has reached the lowest level since 1991. At the end of 2016, it was 8.3% and was lower than that recorded in 2015 by 1,4 percentage points. The number of unemployed registered at the end of 2016 was 228 000. lower than a year ago. The ratio of the number of long-term unemployed to the total of active workers decreased by half between 2013 and 2016, from 4.4% up to 2.2%. Export dynamics. Export dynamics were higher than import dynamics. This resulted in an improvement in the balance of trade. In 2016, the negative current account-to-GDP ratio was -0.3%. and decreased from 2015. However, the forecasts of some macroeconomic parameters were not accurate. The growth rate of gross domestic product was 1.1 percentage points below the forecast rate. The increase in investments was lower than assumed by 14.9 percentage points. The recorded unemployment rate was lower than the forecast by 1.4 percentage points and deflation occurred instead of expected inflation. The average annual rate of gold against the euro was higher than the forecasted rate by 10%. And the US dollar is 5.4%. Among the most important economic factors that affected the failure of the forecasts were uncertainty in international financial markets, low commodity prices and a decrease in investment demand in the country. On the other hand, consumption dynamics were similar to the forecast. Private consumption increased by 3.8% in real terms against the projected growth of 3,7%. This was the highest growth since 2008. The high growth in household consumption has significantly affected economic growth in Poland despite significantly smaller investments. High Seym! Both deficits were below the ceiling laid down in the Budget Act by 15,7% And 30.6%. However, their total volume reached a record amount of PLN 58 800 million. This result was 13 billion PLN higher than in the previous year. The magnitude of the budgetary imbalances thus measured reached 3.2% GDP. This relationship was 0.6 percentage points higher than in 2015. Over the last 10 years, the higher ratio of both deficits to GDP was only in 2010 and was 3.9%. In 2016, as in previous years, the Minister of Finance used legal solutions to demonstrate a lower government budget deficit than the economic nature of the operations carried out. However, their scale has been significantly reduced, among others. by reducing transfers to open pension funds. In 2016, operations distorting the transparency of public finances amounted to PLN 4200 million, while in 2013 and 2015 they amounted to PLN 16 600 million on average. These operations consisted in the financing of the Social Insurance Fund from funds included in the expenditure of the State budget rather than in expenditure. Laws allowed this to happen, although this contradicted the economic nature of the operation. This was done in two ways. Firstly, the Social Insurance Fund received a loan from the state budget of PLN billion. The loan as a return transfer was included in the proceeds rather than in the expenses. However, none of the loans granted to the Social Insurance Fund in recent years have been repaid and most of them were decommitted at the beginning of 2017. Therefore, the initial transfers shown as returnable were de facto non-refundable, similar to grants and therefore the assessment of the NIK should be included in the expenditure. Secondly, the Social Insurance Fund has for years received a refund for the loss of contributions to open pension funds. In 2016, this amount amounted to PLN 3200 million. These transfers were accounted for as they were to be financed from privatisation revenues. However, in 2016, such revenues were virtually non-existent and these transfers were financed by the issuance of treasury securities. According to the Supreme Audit Chamber, non-refundable transfers financed from sources other than privatisation revenues should be reported as expenditure. If these transfers were included in expenditure, the government budget deficit would be PLN 4200 million higher than that shown in the Council of Ministers' report and the government deficit-to-GDP ratio would increase from 2.5% Up to 2.7%. The Supreme Audit Chamber also notes that the deficit of the budget of European funds was not due to the lack of resources provided by the European Commission, but to the decision of the Minister of Finance to use the funds transferred from refunds and advances. Part of the appropriations not allocated to the revenue of the European budget was used in accordance with the internal procedure of the Ministry of Finance to manage the liquidity of the State budget. In 2016, the Minister of Finance, following the requests of the Supreme Audit Chamber, adopted rules limiting the freedom to shape revenue and the financial result of the European budget for the 2014-2020 perspective. However, these rules did not lead to the Minister of Finance's full compliance with the principle of spot budgeting. High Seym! The net lending needs of the State amounted to PLN 57 100 million and were lower than planned by PLN 26 600 million. However, compared to 2015, it increased by 3300 million, i.e. over 6%. The main contribution to the development of loan needs was the deficits of the state budget and the budget of European funds and the aforementioned funds for the financing of the Social Insurance Fund. The financing of loan needs was almost entirely obtained by borrowing debt on the domestic market. The cost of obtaining the funds increased slightly compared to the costs incurred in 2015, i.a. the result of a temporary decrease in the rating of Poland, the outflow of foreign investors from the domestic debt market. The profitability increased despite the fact that the financing of loan needs in 2016 was carried out under the conditions of historically highest free resources collected on gold and foreign exchange deposits. Their daily average level in 2016 amounted to PLN 49 300 million and was above the level maintained in 2015 by PLN 1800 million. Free funds were obtained by issuing debt, which, after deduction of the income generated from their placement, cost the taxpayer more than PLN 220 million. According to the Supreme Audit Chamber, the carrying out of emissions resulting in a significant and long-term increase in the position of investments should be preceded each time by analyses of the accuracy and effectiveness of the acquisition of funds, based on the data as close as possible to the current market situation. The Supreme Audit Chamber, during its audit in the Ministry of Finance, made comments on the analysis to date in this area. The privatisation revenues were made in 2016 amounting to 68 100 thousand. zł. That was 31%. the amount planned for 2016 The lower performance of revenue in 2016 compared to plans and in relation to previous years was the result of the verification of companies intended for privatisation due to a change in the concept of asset management of the Treasury. The total revenue generated has been transferred to certain funds financed from these revenues. There was no revenue to provide full funding for the guarantee and guarantee reserve fund. The State Treasury Minister did not transfer to this account the amount of PLN 99 million due for the years 2015 to 2016, thus failing to comply with the provisions of the Article. 25a Law on Guarantees and Guarantees granted by the State Treasury and certain legal persons. High Seym! The 2016 Budget Act includes financial plans for 29 State special-purpose funds, 38 state-owned legal persons, 10 executive agencies and budgetary institutions. In 2016, for the first time since 2013, state-owned special-purpose funds have reached an excess of revenue over costs. It amounted to PLN 2,300 million. Compared to the previous year, the shortage of Social Insurance Fund decreased slightly by 1.3%. The State budget subsidies accounted for around 26% Total revenues of these units and were 2,4% higher than in 2015. 91% grants were transferred to targeted funds, of which almost 64% - to the Social Security Fund. 2016 was the third year in which the state legal entities achieved a positive financial result. The positive aspect was that it amounted to almost PLN 1 billion, i.e. by more than PLN 110 million more than in 2015 under the conditions of repayment of the fall in funding from budgetary grants. The State budget grants to 33 state-owned legal persons accounted for around 3% realised revenues of all state-owned legal entities. For the first time in 4 years, executive agencies suffered a net loss of over PLN 0.5 billion. This result was lower than that recorded in 2015 by nearly PLN 1500 million. The net loss of the Agricultural Property Stock of the State Treasury was affected by this change, the revenue of the stock decreased by more than 58%. This was the result of the implementation of the law on the suspension of the sale of the property of the Agricultural Property Resource of the State Treasury and on the amendment of certain laws. Moreover, more payments to the State budget were planned for the mandatory burden arising from the Law on the Management of Agricultural Real Estates of the State Treasury. In this situation, the Supreme Audit Chamber has not found grounds for reducing the assessment of the implementation of the financial plan of the resource. Between 2013 and 2016, the institutions of the budgetary economy suffered losses. However, it should be noted that they were gradually reduced from 16 million in 2013 to 9 million last year. The financial plans of the audited out-of-budget economy units were carried out correctly in most cases and the irregularities identified did not have a major impact on the management of public funds. The exception is the financial plan of the Polish Space Agency, which the Supreme Audit Chamber has assessed negatively. The negative assessment was based primarily on irregularities involving 8.6% expenditure incurred by the Agency. There have been irregularities concerning the award of public contracts, management control and implementation of the budget in the Task Force. Part of the tasks set out in the Act on the Polish Space Agency were carried out to a limited extent and the keeping of a single register of space objects was not carried out at all. Three special-purpose funds, which were disproportionately high in relation to the annual costs of carrying out their tasks, also need to be noted at the end of 2016. The cash situation of the Reprivatisation Fund was 23 times higher than the costs incurred by the Fund in 2016. In the Guaranteed Occupational Benefits Fund and the Gambling Problems Fund, the level of funding was 14 times higher than the annual financial needs. In the view of the Supreme Audit Chamber, funds should primarily finance the tasks set out in the laws creating the individual special funds, while noting the specific objective of the Reprivatisation Fund, which may justify the high level of the funds of the Fund. The keeping on the accounts of the other two high-level funds over the long term does not contribute to the effective achievement of the objectives set out in the laws. This adverse phenomenon has already been highlighted by the Supreme Audit Chamber in previous years. The High Sejm, the state public debt calculated according to the national methodology at the end of 2016 amounted to more than PLN 965 billion. It increased by nearly PLN 88 billion compared to the end of 2015. This was the largest nominal debt increase since at least 2003. It was mainly due to the financing of the state budget and the budget of European funds and the depreciation of gold. At the end of 2016, the government's debt-to-GDP ratio was 52.1%. Compared to the previous year, it increased by 3,4 percentage points. The largest share of public debt was the Treasury debt. His debt was 92.6% Total debt. Nearly 2/3 of the Treasury's debt was incurred on the domestic market. Although the share of foreign debt decreased slightly, due to the cost of its handling Poland transferred the amount of PLN 10 billion a year abroad. General government debt determined according to the Union methodology exceeded PLN 1 trillion at the end of 2016. It was higher than at the end of the previous year by nearly PLN 87 billion. Debt-to-GDP ratio increased from 51.1% in 2015 to 54.4% in 2016 Poland was for the first time outside the first ten countries of the European Union with the lowest level of debt in relation to GDP. However, this relationship for Poland was still lower than the value of 83.5% for the European Union as a whole. , and significantly lower than the euro area average of 89.2%. The difference between the state public debt calculated according to the national methodology and the government debt calculated according to the principles established by the EU institutions exceeded 41 billion PLN. It was mainly due to the non-recognition in the sovereign public debt of the National Road Fund. In the past, the Supreme Audit Chamber has called for the harmonisation of the methodology for calculating public debt to include full liabilities of public entities. In this way, the possibility of an uncontrolled increase in sovereign debt would be excluded. High Seym! The statement of revenue and expenditure of the public finance sector is a mandatory part of the government's report on the implementation of the State budget. According to the Supreme Audit Chamber, the revenue and deficit of the public finance sector, as in previous years, have been calculated correctly. The proceeds of the Social Insurance Fund received from the State budget for the contribution refund for open pension funds have not been eliminated from revenue. As a result, the revenues and performance of the public finance sector were overestimated by PLN 3200 million. The correctly calculated deficit in the public finance sector was PLN 49 300 million in the assessment of NIK and was 2.7% in relation to GDP. The Minister of Finance in our assessment has miscalculated the size of this deficit and the volume of public revenue since 2004. In total, the deficit of the public finance sector was reduced by PLN 158 600 million in 2004/2016, i.e. an average of PLN 12 200 million per year compared to the data presented in the reports. This means that this difference was much lower in 2016. In 2016, the tax-to-GDP ratio of the public finance sector was 8.7% and was 0.5 percentage points higher than in 2015. However, this relationship was still much lower than the average in the European Union, accounting for almost 27% GDP. While the scale of tax revenues related to production and import in relation to the size of the economy was similar in Poland to the EU average, the scale of income and property tax revenues was almost half as small. The ratio of income from capital taxes to GDP was also much smaller. This means that in this respect Poland created better conditions for capital investment than most European Union countries. Half of the expenditure of the public finance sector was carried out in three sections: Mandatory social security, health care and education, the first of which was absorbed by almost 1/3 of public funds. Compared with the figures for 2015 and 2016, one can first of all see a very large increase in expenditure in the section: Social assistance, linked to the introduction of the educational benefit from the program The financial situation of local government units was improved. They achieved a record budget surplus of PLN 7600 million. This was mainly due to a 1/3 reduction in property expenditure compared to 2015 levels. This surplus allowed local governments both to reduce their debt levels and to raise funds for future investments. The general government deficit, calculated according to the Union methodology, amounted to 2,4% in 2016 in relation to GDP. GDP. This indicator was 0.2 percentage points lower than a year ago. However, it continued to develop by 0.7 percentage points above the average for all EU countries. The Polish Government has committed itself to reducing it in the following years. In Poland, the general government deficit has not so far been a threat to the financial stability of the State, although the average deficit in 1995 and 2016 was significant and amounted to 4.2%. GDP. Despite this financial imbalance, there was no excess of an acceptable threshold of 60% of public debt. GDP. High Seym! The scale and gravity of the irregularities found in the implementation control of the State budget in 2016 was not significant. In particular, the main irregularities identified as a result of the checks on certain contributors involved: failure to comply with procedures and rules in the award of public contracts, errors in the award of grants and in the supervision of their use, the non-target spending of funds, the conclusion of civil law contracts with individuals of the nature of employment contracts, the delay in the implementation of multiannual programmes which were not intended to be used for the use of appropriations from special provisions, the absence of budgetary duties, the setting of measures to prevent the measurement of the results of the tasks carried out, the insufficient supervision of responsibilities over subordinate units and ineffective management control mechanisms. Following a review of the implementation of the State budget in 2016 The Supreme Audit Chamber has addressed more than 350 proposals to control bodies whose implementation will enable better, more efficient implementation of budgets in the coming years. The Supreme Audit Chamber recognises the need to strengthen the oversight of the budgetary components of the implementation of the financial plans. In this respect, the proposals concerned, in particular, the recovery of claims, the use of grants and the continuation of measures to improve the forecasting of revenues. It is necessary to improve the reliability of the verification of the needs reported by the beneficiaries in the event of the mobilisation of funds from the special reserve. The Supreme Audit Chamber also points to delays in the use of funds from the financial perspective of the European Union for the period 2014-2020. It is reasonable to implement effective management mechanisms that allow for a long-term assessment of the achievement of the objectives and the response to emerging risks. The Supreme Audit Chamber also recognises the need to improve solutions for a comprehensive analysis of the effectiveness of expenditure incurred. It is also reasonable for the Council of Ministers to carry out a detailed analysis of the level of performance of the tasks included in the workflow of expenditure and the reasons for the failure to fulfil part of the tasks and, on the basis of the results of this analysis, to take measures to improve the functioning of public bodies. In the light of the results of the audit, it is important to continue systemic action on: the implementation of further measures to reduce the most serious risks to the collection of budgetary revenue, and the reform of the budgetary system, among others. to strengthen the role of multiannual planning and to increase transparency and transparency in public spending. High Seym! In fulfilling the constitutional obligation, the Supreme Audit Chamber also carried out an audit of the activities of the National Bank of Poland in the implementation of monetary policy objectives. In 2016, for the fourth year in a row, inflation was significantly below the basic monetary policy objective set in the monetary policy assumptions for 2016 at 2,5%. with an acceptable symmetrical deviation interval of 1 percentage point. From January to October, annual deflation was maintained from 1.1% up to 0,2% In November, the annual increase in consumer prices was 0% , and in December, after more than 2 years of deflation, inflation returned at 0.8% per year. Despite these deviations, the Supreme Audit Chamber considered the assumptions to be fulfilled. The Monetary Policy Council has set the inflation target in a flexible way, allowing for a temporary development of the price index of goods and services outside the range of deviations from the target. At the same time, the Council established a flexible path for the return of inflation to a fixed corridor, depending on the reason for the deviations. The decline in prices of energy raw materials in the world, including oil and natural gas prices, i.e. factors outside the sphere of monetary policy, has been determined by the fact that deflation occurred in Poland. In addition, it should be noted that the deflation did not translate negatively into consumer expectations and decisions and that there were no significant economic disturbances associated with this. The Monetary Policy Council as an appropriate period for assessing the effectiveness of monetary policy has identified a 10-year horizon. If you understand the monetary policy objective, it was certain that it would be achieved at the time of establishing the monetary policy objectives for 2016. The average annual inflation would have to be above 14% or a deflation of less than 6% so that the assumptions are not met. Such a scenario was not assumed by the most extreme predictions. The National Bank of Poland has achieved the operational objectives of monetary policy for 2016. The Management Board of the National Bank of Poland assumed to stabilise the liquidity of the banking sector, making effective use of open market operations, deposit and credit operations and the minimum reserve system. The short-term interest rate on the in-bank market was maintained near the reference rate of the National Bank of Poland. The Supreme Audit Chamber did not find irregularities in the application of monetary policy instruments by the National Bank of Poland, nor did it comment on the implementation of exchange rate policy. At the same time, the College of the Supreme Audit Chamber pointed out: the need to continue to act effectively to seal the tax system, remaining despite a slight improvement in 2016 in the difficult financial situation of the Social Insurance Fund and the need to improve and consistently implement instruments supporting the efficiency of public spending. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u43	Thank you, sir. I ask the President of the National Bank of Poland, Mr Adam Glapiński, to present a report on the activities of the National Bank of Poland in 2016.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u44	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In fulfilling my statutory obligation, I submit to the High House a report on the activities of the National Bank of Poland in 2016. According to the NBP Act, this document was submitted to the hands of the Marshal on 30 May 2017. In my speech before the High House, I would like to discuss the most important activities of the NBP last year. By virtue of the article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with the examination procedure referred to in paragraph 1 of this Article. 1 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland has the exclusive right to determine and implement monetary policy. According to the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, the National Bank of Poland is responsible for the value of Polish money. Article 4 is replaced by the following: 3 The NBP Act clarifies the powers set out in the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, stating that the primary purpose of NBP's activities is to maintain a stable level of prices while supporting the economic policy of the government, unless this limits the basic objective of the NBP. The NBP seeks to maintain price stability by implementing the inflation target strategy. This strategy is applied by leading central banks around the world, and it is essential to anchor long-term inflation expectations at the level of the announced inflation target and to adapt monetary policy instruments, mainly interest rates, to the economic outlook and expected price dynamics. Since 2004 NBP pursues its medium-term inflation target of 2,5% with symmetrical variation range of width plus minus 1 percentage point. During this period, the average consumer price dynamics in Poland amounted to 2%, indicating the effectiveness of this strategy in ensuring long-term price stability. The analysis of the causes of the global economic crisis has led to a partial modification of views on the proper way to conduct monetary policy. At present, it is recognised that stabilising inflation at low levels is a very important but insufficient condition for sustainability in the economy. In order to ensure macroeconomic stability, monetary policy needs to be pursued in such a way as to stabilise inflation at medium-term level, while at the same time reducing the risk of the growth of imbalances in the economy, in particular in the financial system. However, this monetary policy requires a flexible approach to the inflation target. A flexible approach to the inflation target is necessary especially when there are shocks in the global economy that strongly affect national price processes. In 2016, such shocks were first the decline in world oil prices and then their increase. As a result of these shocks, price dynamics in the world first decreased to very low - and in some countries even to negative - levels and then increased. At the same time, global GDP has been the lowest in 2016 since the last global financial crisis and, in the case of developing countries, the weakest in 20 years, reducing global demand pressure. In the immediate vicinity of the Polish economy, i.e. in the euro area, however, there was a moderate recovery supported by the European Central Bank's expansive policy. In the United States, the economic situation improved so much at the end of the year that the Federal Reserve raised interest rates. The strengthening of monetary policy in the United States has contributed to an increase in the profitability of government bonds in the world, as well as the outflow of capital from emerging economies and the weakening of their currencies, including gold. In Poland the economic situation was beneficial. Real GDP growth was 2.7% and despite some reduction compared to 2015, it remained close to the dynamics of the potential product. The main growth factor was the growing demand for consumption, which fostered the acceleration of household income growth related to the ongoing recovery in the labour market, as well as the increase in educational benefits. Restoration in the labour market has contributed to a systematic increase in employment and a fall in the unemployment rate which has fallen to the lowest level since 1991 and reached only 8.3% in December. We are currently seeing a continuation of the decline in the unemployment rate, which is currently estimated at 7.2%. In addition, the increase in credit in 2016 and in the first months of 2017 was stable, accompanied by a recovery in the real estate market. The main contribution to the decline in GDP growth in 2016 was the decline in investments linked to the completion of the European Union's financial perspective 2007 The favourable economic situation of Poland's main trading partners and improving the competitiveness of Polish products were conducive to the acceleration of Polish exports, which contributed to the increase of the surplus in foreign trade. As a result, the current account deficit has fallen to the lowest level since 1995 and the total current and capital account balance has remained positive. Stable economic growth was accompanied by low but rising price dynamics. In December 2016, it amounted to 0.8% year to year compared to minus 0.9% year-to-year in January 2016 The negative price dynamics that persisted for most of the year were mainly due to the strong decline in world prices of energy raw materials at the turn of 2015 and 2016. Lower prices of energy raw materials have had a positive impact on the economic situation of both households and most economic operators, and consequently the deflation did not have a negative impact on the economic situation in Poland. The continuing negative price dynamics did not result in households putting aside consumer expenditure and the companies indicated that it had no significant impact on their sales, financial condition and employment. In view of these circumstances, the Monetary Policy Council maintained the NBP interest rates unchanged in 2016, including a reference rate of 1,5%. In its interest rate decisions, the Council used the flexibility of the inflation target strategy, which means that it took into account, on the one hand, the external and transitional nature of the factors limiting price dynamics and, on the other hand, the sustainable pace of economic growth, systematic improvement in the labour market and a steadily growing credit share. Maintaining unchanged interest rates has encouraged price stability while maintaining macroeconomic equilibrium and stability in the financial sector. At the same time, the NBP's historically low level of interest rates supported the economic downturn, which last year was weakened by a reduction in investment due to the temporary reduction in EU funds. This year, the price dynamics have moved closer to the NBP target and economic growth has accelerated after a temporary weakening in 2016. At the same time, employment growth continues and lending continues to grow at a stable rate. In addition, favourable trends in the balance of payments persist. Thus, NBP is effectively carrying out its constitutional and statutory tasks, maintaining price stability and supporting macroeconomic balance in the Polish economy. Mr. Marshal! Mr Fellowships and Mr Fellow Members, The NBP's monetary policy in 2016 was implemented through a set of instruments adopted by the Monetary Policy Council. The conditions on domestic and foreign financial markets did not require the use of custom tools by the National Bank of Poland. The main monetary policy instrument in 2016 was the NBP interest rates. The NBP reference rate was the most important one. This rate, determining the profitability of the open market operations used by the central bank to control the level of liquidity in the banking sector, affected the level of interest rate on the funds made available in the inter-bank market. The NBP's deposit and pawn shops set a range of overnight interest rates fluctuations on the indicated market. The implementation of monetary policy in 2016 was carried out by the National Bank of Poland, as in previous years, under conditions of persistent overliquidity in the banking sector. Its level was reduced in 2016 with the greatest contribution being made by the increase in cash in circulation. The average level of liquidity in 2016 was about PLN 73 billion compared to about PLN 90 billion a year earlier. In 2016, the operational objective of monetary policy was to develop the Polonia rate near the NBP reference rate. This objective has been achieved effectively. The average absolute deviation from the NBP reference rate was 16 basis points. The operational objective of monetary policy was to implement open market operations in the form of money vouchers. With their use, the National Bank of Poland absorbed excess funds held by banks in order to maintain balanced liquidity conditions in the banking sector. The minimum reserve system was instrumental in achieving the operational objective in 2016. It allowed reserve holders in the NBP to use the funds collected in central bank accounts during the reserve period. In 2016, the NBP's deposit and credit operations had a similar role in achieving the operational objective of monetary policy. With their use, the central bank offered banks the opportunity to locate surplus funds or to supplement current liquidity shortfalls for one-day periods with a deposit at the end of the day and a pawn shop loan respectively. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Effective measures for financial stability require knowledge of the current situation of the financial sector and the identification and assessment of the risks at stake. The National Bank of Poland carries out this task by conducting cyclical research and analysis of this sector, as well as cooperating with other state bodies in the creation of legal regulations and taking actions that promote financial stability. The analyses carried out by NBP confirm that the Polish financial system was stable in 2016. The most important segment - the banking sector - was in good shape. The sector as a whole provided loans at a pace similar to nominal GDP growth, which, by supporting economic growth, did not generate imbalances threatening financial stability. The banking sector was characterised by capital equipment appropriate to the level of its activities. The total capital ratio at the end of 2016 was 17,2% against the minimum level of so-called. Pillar I, of 8%. The banks also complied with the additional capital requirements above the pillar and the buffers imposed by the Financial Supervision Authority. In 2016, the profit of the banking sector amounted to PLN 13.9 billion, i.e. by 8.4%. more than in 2015 The increase in profit was largely due to the one-off sale of shares in the Visa Europe payment card organisation. It has allowed it to neutralise the impact on banks' balance sheets of the tax on certain financial institutions since February 2016. In total, banks paid about PLN 3.2 billion of this tax. However, banks face a number of serious challenges in terms of the gradual entry into force of European regulations, which will affect both the profitability of banks and the ability to lend to real-world players at the current pace. The challenge considered in the public debate has also been to accelerate the restructuring of housing portfolios nominated in foreign currency for a long time. In the assessment of the National Bank of Poland, priority should be given to maintaining the stability of the financial system when taking any institutional action on foreign exchange loans. The National Bank of Poland is in favour of restructuring on the basis of voluntary agreements between banks and borrowers. Allow me to return to this topic at the next part of the speech. The cooperative banks are an important element of the national banking sector. The sector continued in 2016 with major changes in organisational structures in 2015 aimed at strengthening the security and stability of the cooperative sector. This purpose is served, among others. the functioning of institutional protection systems, so-called IPS. Currently there are two such systems in which 87% already exist. cooperative banks. However, 74 cooperative banks remained outside the IPS structures at the end of the first quarter of 2017. I would like to stress that the National Bank of Poland has from the beginning supported the creation of institutional protection systems and pointed out the need to participate in them as many banks as possible. The functioning of the IPS, including in particular the liquidity and capital support funds set up in their structures, shall enhance the security of all participants. They thus contribute to improving the stability of the cooperative banking sector and the national banking system as a whole. Moreover, the functioning of cooperative banks within IPS will help to improve their market position by making more effective use of their development potential. Supporting the sustainable development of the cooperative sector is in the interest not only of cooperative bank managers but also of local communities whose need for financial services is met by those banks. This is also important in terms of ensuring the stability of the financial system, which is monitored by the NBP and other institutions in the financial security network. In summary, the National Bank of Poland supports the further integration of the cooperative banking sector in the form of IPS. This seems an indispensable condition for its long-term operation. As far as the cooperative credit and savings banks sector is concerned, in 2016, as in previous years, Sanitation processes were carried out. The capital situation of some banks remains difficult and the sector needs recapitalisation. In 2016, as a result of restructuring operations, three cashiers were taken over by the banks and five were declared bankrupt. It was necessary to involve the Banking Guarantee Fund, which allocated PLN 600 million to the payment of guaranteed funds to depositors from five cash registers, which reduced its resources and caused the rest of the participants to restore the deposit guarantee scheme. Strengthening the resilience of the financial sector to systemic risk materialisation and monitoring its functioning in the context of sustainable economic development are the subject of an assessment of the Financial Stability Committee in the macro-prudential formula. I would like to move on to a broader discussion of the activities of this macro-prudential body and to share with you my observations on this subject. The collegial formula of the committee is a solution resulting from the existing institutional conditions that we have in Poland. This is due to the fact that in Poland, the tasks and competences of actions to maintain the stability of the financial system are legally assigned to as many as four institutions of the financial security network, i.e. Minister of Finance, National Bank of Poland, the Commission for Financial Supervision and the Bank Guarantee Fund. None of the institutions represented in the committee has a mandate or sufficient tools to address systemic risk, but it is the National Bank of Poland that plays a leading role, because it is the President of the NBP who is the chairman of the committee in the macro-prudential formula, and the NBP provides the service of the committee and provides its basic analytical facilities. In this context, micro-prudential supervision is particularly important, as the effective functioning of the financial security network is only possible with close coordination and harmonious cooperation between micro- and macro-prudential surveillance. Focusing on micro- and macro-prudential supervision in the NBP would undoubtedly improve risk surveillance in the national financial system and increase the effectiveness of crisis prevention. It would be easier and more effective to diagnose systemic risk and use supervisory tools that today's CRS-M can only recommend the institutions represented in the committee, but does not affect their final shape and compliance, i.e. compliance with the requirements of the instruments used by operators in practice. At the same time, international experience clearly shows that in a crisis situation, a model in which micro-prudential supervision is located outside the central bank does not work. The location of micro-prudential supervision at the central bank, however, allows to concentrate responsibility, eliminates problems with the exchange of information and conflicts of competence. As a result, such a model ensures efficient and decisive action so that losses can be effectively reduced. These are important reasons why it has now been decided in most European Union countries, including the Banking Union, to entrust the role of micro-prudential supervision to central banks. I have already informed you about this issue on several occasions, and I have spoken in this Chamber. One of the most important topics that the Committee addressed in the first year of its action in the macro-prudential formula was the issue of housing currency loans mentioned above. In August 2016, on my initiative, as Chairman of the Financial Stability Committees, a Working Party was set up in this macro-prudential formula. The risk of monetary housing loans. Its aim was to propose recommendations for solutions aimed at the financial stability of the restructuring of foreign housing loans mainly granted in the first decade of the 21st century. The setting up of a working group took place when the Committee recognised the urgent need to address the issue of foreign currency housing loans, and considered it advisable to actively involve all the institutions of the financial security networks represented in the PSC in this process. It should be noted that the National Bank of Poland is constantly assessing the risk factors associated with the portfolio of foreign currency housing loans. The analysis shows that, economically, the portfolio of these loans does not pose a significant threat to the stability of the financial system. The situation of the vast majority of households that have borrowed currency loans is good and their resilience to further high exchange rate shocks. This is linked to an increase in nominal wages, low interest rates for foreign currency loans and higher income requirements applied. As a result, foreign exchange housing loans maintain high quality and are well repaid. As a result of several months of research and analysis by this working group in January this year, the Committee adopted a recommendation for restructuring the housing portfolio in foreign currency. The Committee rejected the arrangements resulting in the statutory conversion of foreign exchange housing loans at a rate significantly different from the current market rate. The statutory conversion of housing loans, especially time-focused loans, could lead to very significant losses in some banks, would threaten the establishment of claims by bank owners in international arbitration proceedings and would result in pressure to weaken the gold. Cumulation of all these phenomena could in extreme cases jeopardise the financial and macroeconomic stability of Poland. The Committee has advocated solutions that would lead to the restructuring of the foreign exchange housing loan portfolio on the basis of a voluntary agreement between banks and their customers and set out the framework for such restructuring. All possible measures could be taken to encourage settlements between borrowers and banks have been analysed. Given the complexity of the currency loan restructuring process, it was considered that solutions should refer to a wide range of instruments at the disposal of the institutions represented in the Financial Stability Committee, in particular the Minister of Finance and the Financial Supervision Commission. As it would be complicated and long to make all the recommendations for foreign exchange loans, I would only like to quote a few specific recommendations. These include, first, an increase in risk weight and for banks using advanced equivalent models, an increase in the minimum value of the default loss parameter, so-called. loss given default, for foreign exchange exposures secured by mortgages on immovable property, secondly, the establishment of a systemic risk buffer, thirdly, changes in the operating rules of the Credit Support Fund, fourthly, the issue by the KNF of a supervisory recommendation on good practices in restructuring of foreign exchange housing loan portfolios, fifthly, an increase in capital add-ons under the so-called. Pillar II. The institutions of the Financial Stability Committee shall implement successive recommendations. However, this process involves among others. with the need to carry out appropriate procedures as defined in European law and to comply with the necessary vacathio legis in the context of statutory amendments. Part of the recommendation has already been implemented. The rest is at a very advanced, final stage of work. As a result of the actions taken by the relevant public institutions, a legal and regulatory framework is being created to promote the gradual restructuring of the housing foreign exchange loan portfolio in a way that does not jeopardise the stability of the Polish financial system. The voluntary restructuring process should lead to a tangible reduction of the foreign exchange risk of borrowers. As I mentioned, 2016 was the first full year of the Financial Stability Committee's activities in the macro-prudential formula after its formation in December 2015. The Committee met regularly and examined systemic risks and issued recommendations at each meeting. The Committee recommended keeping the countercyclical buffer at 0% in the quarterly cycle. It also issued a recommendation and opinions that were used by the Financial Supervision Commission to identify 12 systemic institutions on the national market and impose appropriate capital requirements on them. The main analytical documents used by the macro-prudential supervisory authority are prepared by the NBP. The report on the stability of the financial system published twice a year and the annual report on the sustainability of the Polish economy. The Committee also receives from the NBP the results of analyses, studies and other documents for the assessment of systemic risk and thus necessary for the implementation of macro-prudential policies. To sum up the work of the Financial Stability Committee in the macro-prudential formula so far, it is worth noting that it has been effectively coordinating and mobilising action at the stage of prevention of systemic risk. The following years will show how appropriate the Committee's recommendations are and to what extent they are respected by the addressees. We will then be able to assess how effective macro-prudential policy is in our Polish institutional conditions. The effectiveness of the central bank's core function of supporting financial stability and crisis management would significantly improve the integration of financial supervision with the National Bank of Poland. Foreign experience shows that after the financial crisis due to the apparent conflict of competence and the weakness of cooperation mechanisms in crisis conditions, there has been an increase in the involvement of central banks in the supervision of the financial system. Such responsibilities are currently carried out by central banks in the 21 countries of the European Union and by the European Central Bank within the framework of the so-called "central banks." a banking union. The most important arguments in favour of placing financial supervision in central bank structures are the central bank's last chance lender function and the possibility of coordinating micro- and macro-prudential supervision in one institution. In order to perform an effective supervisory function, independence is also necessary, which is undisputed in the case of a central bank. Therefore, the gradual merger of the National Bank of Poland and the Financial Supervision Commission seems necessary to me. Mr. Marshal! Mr. Messengers! Gentlemen! One of the main areas of activity of the National Bank of Poland is the management of foreign reserves. At the end of 2016, the official reserve assets of the National Bank of Poland reached the equivalent of EUR 108.1 billion ensuring that the relevant level of commonly used reserve adequacy indicators is maintained. An appropriate level of foreign exchange reserves serves to strengthen the financial credibility of the country in its assessment, among others. international institutions, credit rating agencies and foreign investors. This influences, among others. at the cost of financing on global markets, as well as supporting the stability of the Polish currency. Incidentally, reserves can also be used to support the stability of financial markets under conditions of significant disruption to their functioning. The priority in managing the foreign exchange reserves of the National Bank of Poland is to ensure a high level of security of the funds invested and their proper liquidity. Only after these two criteria have been met, the NBP takes action to increase the profitability of reserves, e.g. expands the investment spectrum with financial instruments that offer potentially higher profitability than the most liquid bonds in the US dollar and in euro. Currently, NBP also invests in British, Norwegian, Australian and New Zealand securities. In 2016, the rate of return on reserves calculated in PLN reached 5,2%. Its relatively high level was primarily due to the strengthening of most reserve currencies against the gold this year, i.e. in 2016. The return rate depends on market conditions: changes in the rate of gold to foreign currencies, prices of investment instruments and profitability. Under the expected acceleration of global economic growth and inflation and the resulting expectations of a gradual departure of central banks from the comodative monetary policy, there is a growing likelihood of a rise in profitability, i.e. a fall in securities prices in global markets. This would adversely affect both the income and the rate of return on foreign reserve management activities. Also, the potential continuation of the appreciation of the golden trend started in December 2016, which is conducive to good moods in emerging markets, as well as national factors, are at risk of creating high so-called. the costs not realised from the valuation of foreign currency resources. Under such circumstances, NBP has established a reserve in previous years to cover possible losses in this respect. The official reserve assets of NBP are also included in monetary gold. Its stock has not changed in recent years and is around 103 t. In 2016, the Council The NBP received a profit of EUR 6,8 million for the investment of gold, i.e. approx. PLN 29.7 million. High Izbo! In 2016, the Council NBP achieved the highest financial result of PLN 9.2 billion over the last decade. The profit generated by NBP was mainly due to the effective management of foreign exchange reserves. This was supported by favourable market conditions in terms of exchange rates and securities prices in foreign financial markets. In accordance with current regulations, 95% NBP's profits feed the state budget and 5% is written off to the bank's reserve fund. 13 June 2017 The NBP therefore transferred PLN 8700 million to the State budget. The independent external auditor examining the annual accounts of the NBP for 2016 issued an opinion without reservation. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! At the end of 2016, the value of cash in circulation in Poland amounted to PLN 187.6 billion and compared to the previous year it was higher by PLN 24.3 billion, i.e. by 14.9%. Low interest rates and increases in household consumer income and expenditure were conducive to an increase in the value of cash in circulation. In 2016 efforts to modernise the security features of circulation banknotes with a nominal value of between PLN 10 and 200 have been completed since 2009. The actions taken over recent years have been aimed at reducing the cost of emissions by improving the sustainability of banknotes and introducing more modern security features than previously used. The last stage of the modernization of banknotes was the introduction into circulation in February 2016 of a nominal note of PLN 200 with improved security features. Another measure leading to a reduction in the cost of emissions, which was also a response to the market demand of the NBP, was the preparation of a new banknote with a nominal value of PLN 500 for circulation. This happened in February this year. The introduction of a new denomination allowed not only to reduce emission costs, but also to improve the management of the strategic stock of banknotes held by NBP, which is currently held in the two highest banknotes denominations. According to the assumptions, the share of 500 PLN banknote in circulation will be relatively low in the coming years. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In 2016, the Council The National Bank of Poland has actively participated in the work taking place in the European Union to strengthen the stability of the financial system. In addition, he actively cooperated with the European Council for Foreign Affairs. The systemic risk, the body responsible for macro-prudential supervision of the Union's financial system, in order to contribute to addressing systemic risks that could jeopardise financial stability in Europe. At the forum of both the World Bank and the European Bank for Reconstruction and Development, the National Bank of Poland worked together with the Ministry of Finance and the Ministry of Foreign Affairs to strengthen Poland's activity and position in both these institutions, which are important partners in the implementation of global, regional and national development policy. In 2016, the Council The NBP, together with the Ministry of Finance, worked on granting Poland access to the so-called. a flexible credit line for the next period. They ended in January 2017 with the decision of the IMF Executive Board to grant Poland access to FCL for the next 2 years. According to the request of the Polish authorities, the amount of funds made available under the FCL was halved compared to those made available during the previous period. Currently, the value of the flexible credit line opened for Poland amounts to about $9 billion US dollars. Since 2010, the beneficiary of the line is the government and NBP operates the line as a financial agent of the government under Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the implementation Further decisions to extend Poland's access to FCL confirm that the IMF highly assesses our economy, its foundations and the potential for further development. At the same time, access to FCL is an additional safeguard for the Polish economy against the negative consequences of materialization of risks that persist in the external environment of our country. 2016 was the first operational year of the Asian Infrastructure Investment Bank, which Poland is a founding member. Representatives of the NBP and the Ministry of Finance worked closely to evaluate the first infrastructure projects financed by the Bank and to formulate recommendations on the strategy and functioning of the institution. Maintaining active involvement in the bank's work contributes to increasing the influence of Poland on its activities and to strengthening our country's position on the international stage, especially in Asian countries. Draft Act on National Resources Real estate is the answer to the conclusions arising from the diagnosis of the housing situation in the country made in the context of the work on the National Housing Programme. Now it is worth pointing out the main aspect of our actions. This is an increase in access to housing for people with income currently preventing the acquisition or rental of housing on a commercial basis; an increase in the capacity to meet the basic housing needs of people at risk of social exclusion due to low incomes or particularly difficult living conditions; an improvement in housing conditions by improving the technical state of housing resources and increasing energy efficiency. The project I am talking about introduces solutions to increase the supply of rental apartments with moderate rents by using property owned by the State Treasury. We propose the use of these properties for realization of housing investments. The National Property Resource set up under this law will not only serve as a real estate institution of the State Treasury and gain revenue from it, but will also contribute to the creation of stable and efficient financing conditions for the instruments adopted under the National Housing Programme. It is created whether it will be created, we hope, the National Property Resource, which will not be an institution that will take over the property of the State Treasury. I dedicate this information to all those who have otherwise understood our intentions to the provisions of the bill in relation to the National Property Resources. The National Property Stock will have a list of real estate and this list will be drawn up on the basis of information provided by the State institutions. Today, a horse with a row of people who will answer, how much property we have in Poland of the State Treasury, e.g. located within the cities which are included in the spatial planning plans as those that are possible for housing or are included in the study as housing, housing.
