The bill says it.]] They will continue to be in the National Property Resources land of the Treasury as its potential still untapped. Because the lack of use of the possibilities that the State has, with land assets, assets in the form of areas where housing is possible, this is not a sin of negligence, this is not a sin of omission, this is more than that. And we have this potential and these opportunities and we want to use it. The National Property Resource will take over those properties for which specific plans will be made, specific concepts of their buildings, and those properties which will be able to be used directly for housing purposes. The National Property Stock will be tendered for, made available in perpetual use of these properties for housing purposes under certain conditions, which will result in us dedicating these projects, as I said, to this social group, to Poles who do not have the credit capacity today, to those who will be able to rent apartments with access to property. Rental of those parts of apartments built in multi-dwelling buildings in areas where perpetual use will be transferred by tender to the entity, to an institution which will present the best, most favourable conditions... These persons will be protected in a special way against excessive rent, against the possibility of prior sale of these apartments, than is specified in the Act, and from other activities of the housing operator, the managers of those properties, which would change the status of the tenant, the tenant who will fulfil his duties and obligations. And these obligations and obligations we understand as the timely payment of certain rents, rents defined in the revised regulation of the Council of Ministers every year. Someone might ask where the profitability of such an undertaking is. We propose that in each of the buildings, some of the housing units, the developer, should be able to use for commercial purposes, to dispose of these housing units, to establish in them a rent according to their own calculations. We also propose that parts of the premises outside the common parts be separated, if necessary, from commercial premises. We're assuming 50%. the area outside the common parts will be reserved for rental with access to the property, with the protection of the tenant, while the second part, i.e. the remaining 50% , will be used by the builder for commercial purposes. Someone will ask why archaic perpetual use. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, the entity that will participate in the tender will not have to spend money on the purchase of real estate, but will cover it, otherwise: it will pay about 20% of the value of the property valued earlier, before the tender is announced, while the remaining parts will be paid in instalments. And this is the benefit for the business, which we believe to be convinced of, makes these proposals attractive in business. The National Property Stock will also be able to sell the real estate in a tender procedure, but each time it has to obtain the decision of the competent minister, the Minister responsible for construction, the consent to sell, the sale of the real estate in a tender procedure, the transfer of the property in a tender procedure. The National Property Resource will ensure that protection is established for tenants who will rent apartments with a specific rent, that is, regulated. He will ensure that the housing operator - here we introduce the concept of a housing operator - whether it is the original entity that has entered into the tender, has built a multi-dwelling building, whether it has settled, sold parts of these properties, whether it will be another entity which, in the purchase transaction chain, is the ultimate owner of this facility, each time he has to comply with the statutory obligations in the context of the protection of the conditions that were specified in the contract: rent and later access to ownership, i.e. transfer of ownership to the tenant. That's a huge guarantee. This guarantees that these properties will not be removed from the property stock of the State Treasury in an uncontrolled manner. Someone asks: Why is the archaic formula of perpetual use used here? Because we have written in the draft law, there is a provision in which it is clearly defined that in case of failure to fulfil obligations penalties are first imposed, and it is severe punishments, because it must always work in two directions. On the one hand, we offer great opportunities to our business partners, but we also expect them to meet the conditions. And it's never going to be worth not meeting the terms of the contract. And if there is ultimately a drastic breach of the contract, it will always be possible to terminate the agreement with the beneficiary of those properties on which we have transferred the right of perpetual use in the tender formula. National Property Resource is a guarantee of the purchase price of the apartment, also after years. These are guarantees based on our in-depth economic analysis. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u218	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! [[Voice from the room: What's the point?]] So I'm gonna start with the question of statements. First thing, KZN is not a company. As we know, the legal person is not a company, the company is k.s.h., the Commercial Companies Code. The legal person is not a company, it is - like today - the Military Property Agency or the Agricultural Property Agency of the Treasury. That's the first thing. So at this point, there's no company created. As far as I'm concerned, this company and the passwords that were coming here, we are creating an entity that will effectively manage assets in the form of State Treasury real estate, so it is the good of all of us. We're talking about a team of 50 people, maybe 50, depending on how many of these investments are going to go. That's another line. If you're talking about what the impact is, what it's going to look like, it's going to be self-playing, it's going to be an instrument, it's going to be a market mechanism. We're not setting up another entity here, a state-owned company that's going to build. We define a residential operator. The apartment operator is anyone who wants to build apartments under statutory conditions, half of which can be sold immediately and, in short, get a market price today, and half is to be allocated to those people who do not have an adequate salary for rent with a normal rent, that's what we call it. The lease is the basic formula. It was also here that we didn't define the lease. Of course, we define the rental even in the dictionary at the beginning, both the rental and the rental with the option of access. So every tenant for a period of 15 years has the protection of the rent guaranteed by the state. At the same time, any tenant within 15 years of lease, if he decides that he is comfortable with these conditions, may decide to convert this lease into a lease with the option of accessing the property. Then he becomes actually a user, the owner of this place. And for 30 years of lending the statutory system - you can say: the state through this entity, the National Property Resources - guarantees it the rent and redemption of this apartment. So this mechanism is preserved, we have this market mechanism. This was the case here with issues relating to the rubble of developers or economic operators today. There is no such mechanism, this is an additional tool. You want to build on your own territory that you have. You want the state to launch a mechanism for building apartments on the State Treasury land, that is, on the lands that are all of us today, are our property. If we find that there is a housing deficit, what is more, the construction of apartments is our national good and a lesson from history to be done, I think - also from the point of view of your speech, it has been declared - that we are giving these properties to the State Treasury in perpetual use so that none of them can be found, so to speak, a clever entity who will acquire this property and then build nothing on it. And then there's no holding. Then we can count only on God's providence that, in short, he will do whatever we want. In a situation where we put in perpetual use, we have a hold. You can't do what needs to be done, we're taking the real estate. So we keep the goal and this goal is at this point the 30-year period, when those who rent or decide to rent with the option of access to the property - half, because the first half can be disposed of on the trunk, more so, attractive issues concerning e.g. The size of apartments or equipment... This is a market mechanism and this country guarantees. Here - that is also what Mr Khrushcz asked - after this period it will be possible to transform perpetual use into property, and this mechanism has therefore been preserved in order to have this statutory maintenance and influence of the legislator on the possible actions of an unreliable entity, which at this point will commit itself to enter this programme, of course in the form of building apartments. That's another line. There was a talk at the beginning, as I also mentioned, about the transfer of apartments. There's nothing going on here from the vending machine. The first thing for me is: any entity holding the State Treasury's land today should have a list, which is logical for me. I have a list of what I have at home. I know how many furniture I have right now, how many cars I have, how many properties I have. And that's the same thing. The entities that today hold the State Treasury's property entrusted with the management, let them show how many of them are, with a focus on their use for housing purposes. So we point out the administrative boundaries of cities or local plans or studies that are being transferred to housing. So we don't want, in short, any other terrain, we show you what we have first. Then it's Art. 13, Art. 15, Art. 20 of this law, I encourage you to read, say explicitly that the entity, i.e. the President of the National Property Resource, which - as indicated - must be an entity consisting of experts who know the real estate market, says this: from this list XYZ I choose the properties I would like to get. And only as he points out with his finger - this, this and this property - after analysing the economic conditions, location conditions, the conditions of the potential clientele, i.e. buyers, are present to the entity that collected these properties to pass on these properties. So there is nothing from the vending machine that will in any way take away from local governments that have real estate for perpetual use, or from these three entities, I am talking about the State Forests, the Agricultural Property Agency or the National Agricultural Support Centre, from September 1st will be the name, and the Military Property Agency. This formula is clearly written. Secondly, just as you put the property into the resource, which I say doesn't happen from the vending machine, you can take it out the same way. There's no formula at this point that's gonna cause that once you put it in, you're not gonna take it out, and everything you show us, we're gonna take it away. Here is the logical point. This is also to be a good tool for efficient asset management when it comes to building housing. There were also questions about the location. Ladies and gentlemen, as it happens, if we look only at the administrative boundaries of the cities, it's 350,000. ha, as far as the State Forests are concerned, and more than 50,000. ha agricultural real estate is within the administrative boundaries of cities. And now that we have this potential at this stage of knowledge, I think it's definitely from this potential, of those over 400,000. ha we will choose several dozen hectares, which will be suitable in the first approach to localization - I note, in a good place - these housing investments. Because the essence of this program is not to build on the outskirts that we build on a good case today without communication, without access to the necessary infrastructure etc. There was a question about using these properties to build apartments. We clearly point out that on those properties that will be transferred to the resource, we will also want to build accompanying infrastructure, i.e. basic media, which, for example. There isn't any today. Often there is no such correlation despite existing local plans for housing and it turns out that the so-called. Water pipe or sewer pipe doesn't work. These issues will also be pointed out in this formula. One of the information that was duplicated here, which I would like to correct, was information on the increase in the number of apartments per rent. As I'm pointing out, this rental is defined here - what is an apartment for rent, what is an apartment for rent with an option to access property. So we're balancing this market. Of course, we are looking at the labour market because people are today, so to speak, mobile and often moving from the city of X to the city of Y. We want the housing portfolio to be as wide as possible for everyone. The next thing you know, it's the rent thing. As mentioned here, the regulation of the Council of Ministers will determine the amount of rent depending on the location, whether it is a voivodeship city, whether it is a metropolitan area or a capital city or a district. We indicate maximum rent barriers - maximum rents which must not be exceeded and for which the entity will be cleared if it decides to exceed them, by imposing an administrative penalty which constitutes the revenue of the State Treasury and not the legal person. And these rents are kept. The example here is the city of Pomerania. This rent, as we look at, would be about 500 PLN according to our calculations. That, I think, for at least 50 meters apartment isn't rent, so to speak, sounded. Another question, too, was the question of the developer, his possible bankruptcy. We have all kinds of safeguards that speak directly of it. If there is an entity today who reports bankruptcy, then his successor enters into his shoes, in full, with all kinds of sharpenings, with all kinds of promises he made, so that there are no such situations that we have often observed that the building entity is falling, and then people are either left without anything, or are often doomed to lose property that they have purchased for hard-earned money. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u228	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Dear Applicants! I do not share most of the opinions that you have made, but the subject of my speech is the citizens' proposal for a law on conditions of employment in health care, Parliamentary Printing No 1648, and I will refer to this as the subject of our debate today. The project lists those forms of employment and occupations, which include a proposal for a change in the level of employment. The employment rate of the various professional groups was adopted in the draft law according to the labour rate - I do not really understand what that means - from three coefficients to one, and for those who do not require secondary education, the remuneration rate is set at 0.75. According to the draft, the minimum basic salary will be the product of the basic amount and the labour factor included in the Act. The basic amount used in the Act shall be the average remuneration within the meaning of Article. 1 point 3a of the Act on minimum remuneration in the year preceding the establishment of the lowest remuneration. It is worth noting that the average salary in June 2017 according to the Central Statistical Office amounted to PLN 4508,08. The draft law states that if a worker does not receive a remuneration of the amount specified by the law, the medical practitioner is to increase his salary to the minimum within 3 years, starting in July 2017, at 33,33% the amount which is the difference between the lowest basic salary and the remuneration which the employee is entitled to receive under that law. However, the bill has significant shortcomings, contains inaccuracies, lack of precision in defining professions and many key concepts. In the law there is no definition of the concept of labour factor, which is crucial for determining the amount of benefits. There is no reference in the draft to salaries in other non-medical professions, no reference to the scale of expenditure from public funds and from employers' health measures. In the project there is also no reference to the median salary, which is 2800 PLN, i.e. the salary most frequently paid in Poland in 2017, there are no comparisons with other professions with similar level of education and qualification requirements. The project does not contain an assessment of the financial consequences of the implementation of the Law, does not indicate the sources of financing for its implementation costs. The title of the Act - on employment conditions in health care - is very broad. However, the text of the Act itself concerns one of the conditions of employment, in the lowest declaration and in fact a very high remuneration. No one is questioning the need for changes in the pay system in Poland, including compensation in the health care system. I myself wrote books on this subject and have many publications, where I show that wages in Poland are on average 3 and 4 times lower than in the developed countries of the European Union. I would also like to point out that there has been a very tedious process of raising wages. However, it cannot grow suddenly and only for some groups. And now my request after reading this law, after a thorough study of the justification - I will not refer to it because my time is running out here - is as follows: the financial consequences should be assessed by the Minister of Finance before proceeding in committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u286	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Yesterday at the meeting of the Committee on Energy and the Treasury, the first reading of the parliamentary draft law on the amendment of the Renewable Energy Sources Act took place. The adoption by the committee in the vote of the draft law took place after a long discussion and no amendments were tabled during the process of the draft. The amendment of the RES Act has been prepared to stimulate the green certificates market and to close down the certificates in the longer term in that market. In the proposed law, we change the amount of the unit substitute fee by amending the article. 56 points 1 and 2 of the RES Act. Point 1 introduces a new formula for the calculation of the replacement fee, point 2 establishes the need to publish an annual price weighted on the basis of the arithmetic average of the annual prices weighted where those prices have been published by more than one entity referred to in Article. Article 64 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 RES Act. In Amendment 2 to Draft Print Act No 1733 concerning Art. 64 in paragraph 2. 5 a new wording is given to the first sentence, which is the consequence of the introduction of the above-mentioned text. an amendment on how to calculate the substitute fee. Art. 2 The amendment concerns the entry into force of the proposed regulation 30 days after the date of publication. The general objective of the amendment is to introduce solutions to the RES Act, which contribute to the more sustainable development of Poland in the area of renewable energy sources. Therefore, we propose to link the amount of the unit substitute fee referred to in Article. 56 RES Act, with market prices of property rights resulting from certificates of origin. Applicants take the view, and this is also the justification for the proposed amendments, that without additional intervention measures on the part of the legislator it is not possible to reduce the current surplus certificates, which is currently estimated at around 20TWh. This change seems to address the problem of increasing interest of RES generators in the stock market. The point is that the replacement fee currently set at the quota amount has already lost its ability to boost the regulatory environment and will potentially increase costs for end-users in the longer term, all the more so as the so-called increase may occur. the green obligation, in accordance with the RES Act, up to a maximum of 20%. Importantly, the proposed change in the method of calculating the replacement fee for the end-user is completely neutral. Finally, I would like to emphasise once again that the motivation of the applicants to submit an amendment to the RES Act, which we are talking about, which is the subject of today's debate, is above all concern about the final consumer of energy, i.e. the ordinary citizen, the consumer of energy. The aim is to encourage players on the market of certificates of origin to sell certificates in a transparent manner through a commodity energy exchange, while at the same time providing them with greater profits due to the creation of RES, while at the same time the long-term benefit that ordinary electricity consumers in Poland can benefit from in the form of guaranteeing them a stable price for electricity collection. I think that in a sufficient way - perhaps not - I have put forward the views of the applicants and their arguments behind the amendments to the RES Act. But if not, of course I am available as rapporteur for the committee, as are the representatives of the Ministry of Energy present here, who are well aware of the issue of the market for certificates of origin and who fully sign up for this draft amendment to the RES Act. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u287	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. I ask Mrs Ewa Kozanecka to speak on behalf of the Law and Justice Club. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u288	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I present my position on the parliamentary draft law amending the Renewable Energy Sources Act, print No 1733. The aim of the proposed law is to introduce a solution to facilitate the sustainable development of our country in the area of renewable energy sources by changing the way in which the unit replacement fee is calculated, which is an element of making the green certificates market more flexible and, in the long term, reducing the over-supply of certificates in this market. What's the status? In a certificate-based, i.e. property rights, RES support system, renewable energy producer in addition to energy sales receives a certificate for every megawatt hour, which is a property right and can be sold either on the stock exchange or in an individual OTC transaction, i.e. over-the-counter. The sale price is a support for the manufacturer. On the other hand, energy sellers for end-users must license themselves with a quantity of certificates corresponding to their sales volume, i.e. they must buy them. Alternatively, they can pay the so-called. a replacement fee of PLN 300,03 per megawatt hour, which is significantly different from the market prices of property rights. For example, the average price of property rights referred to in Article. For the purposes of this Regulation, the following definitions shall apply: Point 8 (2) of the RES Act, in force in June, amounts to PLN 31.56 and the average annual price in force in 2017 amounts to PLN 73.63. What do we propose? The fundamental change proposed in the project is to abandon the constant value of the so-called. the replacement fee and its link to market prices of energy certificates of origin from green RES certificates and blue certificates, with the assumption that the replacement fee itself will be calculated separately for these producer groups. It should be noted here that the green obligation itself is also separate for agricultural biogas and separate for other producer groups. The fee would be 125%. the average price of the certificates in the previous year but not more than PLN 300,03 per MWh. This means that the method of calculating the replacement fee is changing, but - what is important - it does not change the maximum possible value, which is constant and is 300.03 PLN per megawatt hour. Currently, surplus certificates of origin so-called. green certificates are estimated to be around 20TWh. At present, the surplus of certificates is so high that, without additional intervention by the legislator, it is not possible to close it down. The amendment will also constitute a structural element of the support scheme for RES producers based on the obligation to obtain and present a certificate of origin for redemption, including certificates of origin for agricultural biogas. The change in the method of calculating the replacement fee proposed in this project for the final consumer will be neutral. In the current model, the permanent replacement fee loses citizens, as nearly 60% of turnover in the market for certificates of origin takes place outside the stock exchange at a price of PLN 300,03 per MWh. The analysis of the price of certificates of origin just a few days ago is so that 97% transactions took place outside the stock market at an artificially fixed price of PLN 285.03 per MWh by the traders of certificates of origin, whereas only around 3% of the transactions took place at market price, i.e. 27 PLN per MWh. The final cost was certainly borne by the final recipient. They also lost RES companies and profit from RES producers who, on the one hand, complain that they are suffering losses due to the low value of the RES certificate and, on the other hand, artificially, because in a non-market way, they determine the prices of certificates of origin. It is therefore urgent to stabilise this market so that the end-user does not bear such excessive costs. The legislative solution analysed does not raise any objections in the light of constitutional conditions, EU law or international agreements obligations. The draft law is compatible with European Union law. It is proposed to enter into force 1 month after its publication. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of the parliamentary bill amending the Act on Renewable Energy Sources. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u304	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the proposal for a parliamentary bill amending the Renewable Energy Sources Act, print no. 1733. The aim of the proposed law is to link the unit replacement fee with the market prices of property rights resulting from certificates of origin for energy from renewable energy sources defined inter alia. as green certificates. This solution will make the certification market more flexible and, in the long term, reduce their oversupply by changing the amount of the unit replacement fee as a structural component of the system of support for renewable energy sources. The payment of a replacement fee is an alternative to the obligation to redeem energy certificates. Energy sellers can also do their duty otherwise by paying a substitute fee to the state fund. However, apart from a few exceptions, they do not do so. This mechanism has been permanently blocked by the conclusion of long-term contracts with the so-called. Sewn energy price levels of green certificates. These agreements have also become attractive collaterals for loans provided by banks for investment in RES. Instead of giving the opportunity to buy a cheap certificate on the stock exchange, non-sessional transactions with very expensive certificates are carried out today at prices from PLN 200 to PLN 300, when the replacement fee is currently PLN 300.03 per megawatt hour and green certificates can be bought today on the stock exchange for less than PLN 30. [[Voice from the room: 20 PLN.]] Twenty-four zlotys, that's right. Upon entry into force of the amendment, the replacement fee will be 25%. more than the market price of certificates from the last year, as in 2016 green certificates cost around 74 PLN. After the entry into force of the Act, the substitute fee should fall from PLN 300.03 to PLN 90.95 per megawatt hour. Thus, there will be an impulse to buy cheaper certificates on the stock exchange. Demand will begin to emerge, resulting in a slow increase in certificate prices. This will also reduce the market oversupply of certificates, which currently amounts to more than 22.5 TWh. However, there may be some danger for investors of renewable energy sources who have previously concluded long-term contracts with sellers obliged to sell certificates. It is likely that it may be difficult for both parties to renegotiate contracts or, worse than that, to settle disputes on a judicial basis on the basis of the provisions of the Civil Code, in particular Article. 357 The Civil Code, in which the rebus sic stantibus clause was formulated. It will be a long and difficult process. Some investors in the RES sector, especially smaller ones, may face a loss of liquidity in their businesses as a result of the entry into force of the Act and lose the possibility of repaying loans to finance the construction of renewable energy installations. But there may also be problems for banks, including those of the state that lent these investments. The beneficiary of the Act will become mainly energy companies with the participation of the State Treasury, primarily ENERGY, but also Tauron, Enea, PGE, which concluded in previous years price-deficit long-term contracts, whose prices differ significantly from market prices. Today, the differences between contractual and market prices must be paid out of their own pockets, often hundreds of millions of zlotys per year. It is important that this law does not create chaos on the market and finally introduces a very expected stabilisation for all its participants. I would like to ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Environment, Mr Sławomir Mazurk, to present the justification for the bill. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u338	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The draft law amending the Packaging and Packaging Waste Management Act and some other laws aims to bring Polish law into line with the provisions of Directive 2015/720 of the European Parliament and of the Council. It refers to reducing the consumption of lightweight plastic shopping bags. The main amendment of the current legislation on packaging and packaging waste contained in the draft law is the introduction of provisions on so-called. recycling charges. These provisions transpose the Directive under the option chosen by Poland to adopt instruments ensuring that, from 1 January 2018, light plastic bags will not be offered free of charge at points of sale of goods or products. The following measures have therefore been adopted in the framework of the transposition of Directive 2015/720. A recycling charge was introduced to be charged by the retail or wholesale sales entity to the customers of a lightweight plastic shopping bag. The maximum rate of charge may not exceed 1 PLN and the applicable rate will be determined after public consultation in the Regulation of the Minister of the Environment. If a certain fee rate does not reduce the consumption of plastic bags, the Minister of the Environment will be able to increase the current rate no more than the maximum amount specified in the Act, i.e. 1 PLN. Very light plastic shopping bags, so-called. sampling shall be exempted from the recycling levy provided that it is required for hygienic reasons or offered as a basic packaging of food in bulk if such action prevents food from being wasted. Directive 2015/720 requires Member States to report to the European Commission on the annual consumption of lightweight plastic carrier bags per capita. The Minister for the Environment will draw up such reports on the basis of information on the impact of the recycling levy. In addition to the transposition of Directive 2015/720, the draft law also provides for changes resulting from the assessment of the application of the provisions of the Package Management and Packaging Waste Act in force since 2014. These changes are primarily orderly in nature and the most important of them are the exclusion from the application of the provisions of the Act of Entrepreneurs who, during a given calendar year, have placed on the market products in packaging and subsequently made exports or intra-Community deliveries of these packages or products in packaging; the inclusion of the organisation of the economic self-government, i.e. agreements between the Marshall of the Province and traders marketing products in multi-material packaging or dangerous products in packaging among entities which may request documents for the recovery of waste and recycling; the clarification of the provisions setting out the deadline for the application and issuing of DPO and DPR documents and the clarification of the rules for those documents in respect of packaging waste from households; the clarification of the rules concerning the external audit of economic operators issuing recovery and recycling documents primarily within the scope of the audit competence of the auditor and the consequences for the audit of irregularities; the introduction in Annex 2 to the draft bill of models for the calculation of the product charge taking into account the obligation for the organisation to clear the recovery of packaging of packaging from households. It's about recovery, 50% recycling. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The provisions proposed in the draft law transposing the Directive are intended to reduce the use of lightweight plastic carrier bags. In Poland, according to estimates, each resident consumes about 250-300 plastic bags of various kinds. The life cycle of such a bag is extremely short and is usually several minutes long, which is actually how much time it takes to get home from shopping. Foil bags then become waste that pollutes the environment, including the aquatic environment, threatening many species of animals, including marine animals. This fallout is extremely difficult to remove from the environment, while the experience of other European Union Member States indicates that this solution, i.e. the introduction of bag fees, effectively discourages the use of these bags and reduces this use by up to 90%. In turn, additional amendments to the current legislation have been introduced in order to clarify them and facilitate their practical application and to prevent non-compliant activities from being applied by entities operating in the waste management market. Thank you very much for your attention. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u339	Thank you very much, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u340	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! This law is indeed a transposition of part of the provisions of EU directives into Polish law. The Minister has talked more about these lightweight plastic packages here. Indeed, according to what is happening in other European countries, it is worth introducing fees, some of which will go to the state budget and can then be allocated to environmental activities, but above all will encourage Poles to use reusable or greener packaging, such as paper or materials. This provision also allows the use of very light bags for packing bulk products or those for which hygiene obliges the use of such bags. However, a significant part of the provisions of this law concern the clarification and sealing of the provisions of the existing legislation on packaging and packaging waste management. Entrepreneurs themselves in this area have pointed to a huge gray area and the need to clarify the rules so as to eliminate entrepreneurs who are unfairly acting in this market. What has the bill done in this area? First, the reporting provisions are clarified. Entrepreneurs at committee meetings in discussions with Members have often pointed out that there are many actors who report, which have little to do with reality. Therefore, the possibility of greater control of these reports should, firstly, contribute to the elimination of unfair entrepreneurs from the market and, secondly, to the fact that we will eventually start to meet those recovery and recycling standards which impose on us The European Union, and in the near future. Another element is to settle the introduction of packages according to the same type of packaging and not only by weight. For all we know, it led to such pathology, that e.g. wood became the type of packaging product that was accounted for and replaced by all other types of products, although wood is in fact not so often used. Another element is to clarify the obligation to conduct public educational campaigns and possible penalties for not carrying out such campaigns. That's the element that's been missing so far. Another element that facilitates reporting is the voivodship marshal and the Minister of the Environment will publish in the Public Information Bulletin information about agreements and objectives achieved as a result of these agreements, and if they do not achieve these objectives, then it will be possible to calculate the product charge required for individual entrepreneurs. Another element that has so far failed concerns an external audit obligation in an undertaking which has not often been performed. In practice, the trader who did not carry out this audit could not have been punished. Here are the rules that give the marshal weapons in this case: not only penalties, but even the possibility of removing from the company register, which works in this way. It will be easier for entrepreneurs to switch from the form of paper reporting to the form of electronic reporting, which is agreed with the Ministry of Digitalisation. The definition of the marshal in charge was also amended. checks on undertakings introducing packaging or subsequent clearing on behalf of the introducing undertakings. Here the marshal of the voivodship, where such activity takes place, will be the competent marshal. This is intended to facilitate control of recovery and recycling activities in the voivodship. Until now, when the company was in another part of Poland, this was significantly difficult. It is also about the possibility for economic self-government organisations to reach agreement with marshals on the relevant documents and the question of representing companies introducing products in packaging. Exporters who export goods outside the Union or sell them to the Community shall also be facilitated. Then part of the reporting obligations will be removed, which should facilitate their activities. And the elements of punishing dishonest entrepreneurs. Some penalties have been reduced, in particular those related to the introduction of a new recycling levy on plastic bags. Here punishments have been greatly reduced, even several dozen times, [[♪ The bell ♪]] Whereas higher penalties have been introduced for those traders who cheat, act illegally and instead of making recovery and recycling, actually often bury these goods or repeatedly turn the same papers. This law should contribute to the normalisation of this market, it should be implemented as soon as possible. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u344	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour and pleasure to present the position of the Kukiz club15 on the Act amending the law on packaging and waste management. The formal arrangements of the Act are based on the need to adapt Polish legislation to the rules in force in the European Union and result from an analysis of the application of the provisions of the Act adopted in mid-2013 and in force since 1 January 2014. The legal solutions adopted in the Act aim to reduce the consumption of lightweight plastic carrier bags, but also to control the use, recovery and recycling of packaging. Therefore, the scope of the amendments concerns inter alia.First, the introduction of a recycling charge for lightweight plastic bags. This fee will be charged by the trader operating the retail or wholesale unit in which such bags are offered. Although this is the right solution, it may raise doubts about the amount of such a recycling charge. It can be assumed that it will not be small as it will constitute the revenue of the state budget. Monitoring of the collection of recycling charges will be carried out by the State Trade Inspection; secondly, the extension of the catalogue of cases in which the provisions of the Act do not apply when packaging products are placed on the market. The entrepreneur will not have to apply the provisions of the Act in the field of e.g. ensure the recovery and recycling of packaging waste in respect of packaging in which products have been exported from the country; thirdly, to specify a provision allowing the Marshall of a given voivodship to control the operator's activities in the recovery and recycling of packaging waste. This obligation will apply to the Marshal of the voivodship, where such activities are carried out, not to the Marshal of the voivodship, where the seat of the entrepreneur is located. A number of new definitions are also introduced into the Act, which will make packaging and packaging waste management more rational and more effective. These changes concern, among other things, the new meaning of the term of the public educational campaign. Namely, it is added that this campaign also covers information activities on waste arising from plastic bags. The Act also clarifies the way in which public educational campaigns are carried out on its own. Secondly, the amendment to the definition of dangerous substances refers to the classification, packaging and labelling of dangerous substances. It aims to approximate the legislative, regulatory and administrative provisions of the Member States of the European Union. Provisions relating to the calculation of the product charge are also relevant in the case of failure by the placing on the market of the products in the packaging of the organisation for the recovery of the necessary data to comply with its statutory obligations. An undoubted weakness in this matter is the obligation on the organisation of economic self-government to report separately for each entrepreneur that it represents. It also gives businesses the possibility to keep electronic records on the consumption, recovery and recycling of packaging. The Poselski Kukiz Club15 is in favour of further proceding the bill in committee. Thank you.

Entrepreneurs fear that they will have too little time to set up a system for the settlement of monthly recycling charges and for the modification of spot systems. In the context of the public consultation, a number of comments were submitted to the project, only a small part of which was adopted. Although the project is not perfect, we are ready to support it, because the provisions transposing this EU directive into Polish law should have been implemented long ago. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u366	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The amendment of the Law of 21 December 2000 on technical inspection is necessary in order to implement the European Union Directive, which introduced an obligation to test the efficiency of the fuel vapour recovery systems used in filling vehicles at service stations. During the 6th term of office of the Sejm, work was started on the parliamentary draft law on this matter in 2010, but the legislative work was not completed in connection with the end of the term of office of the Sejm. The way in which the technical equipment of the fuel vapour recovery plant under construction law has been supervised so far does not ensure that the obligations related to the control of their operation are properly fulfilled. It should be noted that these activities require expertise, the ability to operate equipment, as well as interpretations obtained during the performance checks. Therefore, the solution proposed by the government is appropriate, i.e. entrusting the technical supervision units with the activities related to the control of these installations. Technical inspectors shall have the necessary competence, technical knowledge and experience in this field. They are currently carrying out technical supervision over various technical devices installed at fuel stations. The transfer of these additional powers to technical oversight units of fuel vapour recovery equipment is a very effective solution. It is also very important that this new legal solution will certainly have a very positive impact on environmental protection, on the reduction of emissions of harmful vapours. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the draft law amending the Law on Technical Dosor. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u367	Thank you very much, sir. I would ask Mr Józef Lassota to speak on behalf of the Civic Platform Club. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u368	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I have the honour to present my position on the draft law amending the Law on Technical Dosor, original printing No. 1731. The amendment of this law is due to the need to implement the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of Europe on the recovery of fuel vapours at stage two when filling motor vehicles at service stations. These documents require monitoring at the fuel stations of the vapour capture equipment which is released when the vehicles are refueled and transferred to the storage tank. The bill provides for the introduction of system-based solutions for controlling emissions of pollutants associated with air leakage of atmospheric fuel vapours when refueling these vehicles at fuel stations. Given that the relevant technical surveillance units are already carrying out supervision of the technical equipment installed at the fuel stations, that they have resources and competence, and technical experience, it is proposed to entrust this function to the Technical Supervision Office at civil stations, while in the case of fuel located in railway areas, sidings, port areas, seaports and inland navigation is entrusted to the Transport Technical Supervision Authority and, in the case of the army, to the Military Technical Supervision. The proposed provisions require the operator to have a document confirming that the fuel vapour recovery device complies with the minimum requirements for the vapour recovery level. This is not a completely new obligation, as it is already the result of the provisions of the Energy Minister's Regulation on technical conditions to be met by the bases and stations of liquid fuels, long-distance pipelines for the transport of oil and petroleum products and their location. The Civic Platform Parliamentary Club supports the draft amendment of this law. Thank you.

We proceed with the examination of item 24 of the agenda: the report of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on the Government's draft law amending the Animal Health Act and combating infectious animal diseases (prints 1732 and 1746). I ask Mr Krzysztof Szulowski to submit a report to the committee. You're welcome, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u382	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on the Government's draft law amending the Animal Health Act and combating infectious animal diseases, prints 1732 and 1746. The Act is extraordinary in nature. The main objective of the amendment of the Law of 11 March 2004 is the need to clarify the legal provisions applicable to the control of infectious animal diseases and to identify the additional measures necessary for the effective control of those diseases. The need for amendments to the existing legislation is due to an analysis of the activities carried out so far and the current epizootic situation in the area of African swine fever, i.e. new outbreaks and cases in the eastern regions of Poland. The proposed amendments concern rules for the control of infectious animal diseases. The first concerns the imposition on hunters and tenants or managers of hunting circuits of an infectious disease among free-living animals of obligations similar to those of animal owners, including among others. providing information on a significant number of sudden falls, preventing bystanders from accessing animal carcasses and providing information, explanations and necessary assistance to veterinary inspection bodies. The introduction of these measures is intended to prevent the possible spread of the disease and is intended to allow for a faster location of the disease and the collection of relevant epizootic data. The second amendment allows the district veterinarian to order the holder of the holding, by administrative decision, to take action to enable hunters to carry out wild boars in a given area or to prohibit measures preventing or obstructing the execution of such a shot. This solution will help to improve the management of wild boar's sanitary fire, especially in areas where agricultural areas are located in close proximity to the forest. Another change is due to the need to order certain activities in the area of another county. Those provisions aim at effective and rapid decision-making and action by veterinary inspection bodies by impact on entities directly involved in the implementation of disease control measures, including the implementation of rapid and concrete measures to combat outbreaks of infectious diseases, taking the measures set out in the administrative decision, such as the organisation of the transport of dead and killed animals, their disposal and the slaughter of animals and possible processing of their meat in order to inactivate the infectious agent. The provisions concerning the disinfection mats by public road managers are further detailed. The conditions and the manner in which disinfection mats are maintained in the event of the issuing of the order shall be determined in the framework of the agreements of the principal veterinarian with the public road managers. Appropriate road safety in disease areas is one of the measures aimed at limiting the possibility for vehicles to pass the disease through the wheels outside those areas. The catalogue of activities that may be prescribed by regulations issued by the district veterinarian and voivode respectively shall be extended. This provision will allow for a sampling order, in particular by aquaculture operators and owners or managers of hunting circuits, to combat infectious animal diseases. This entails the need for staff to carry out their qualifications. entities. The catalogue of measures which may be available to the district veterinarian in the control and prevention of the infectious disease of animals subject to the control and prevention obligation is extended, namely the prohibition on the holding, rearing or rearing of animals of susceptible species. Experience has so far shown that small farms where a small number of animals are kept for their own use are unable to adapt to the existing bioinsurance requirements due to lack of financial resources or organisational capacity. The Act also introduces significant changes to improve animal health firing in areas covered by nature conservation or in shelters. The issue of such a firing shall be clarified where action is needed to identify further cases of contagious animal disease in free-live animals to shoot off sick or suspected animals of an infectious disease. Sanitary discharge will not be applied to protected animals. A district veterinarian may request that additional persons be designated for the execution of this order by the Polish Hunting Union for individual tenants or managers of hunting circuits. In case of non-execution by the tenants of hunting circuits of certain orders, including in particular the order for sanitary fire, the Polish Hunting Union or the district veterinarian may apply to the Regional Director of the State Forestry Farm of State Forests or the Old Forests for termination of the agreement to lease the Hunting District. This is intended to contribute to ensuring that the prescribed sanitary discharge is effectively carried out by the tenants in order to eradicate the infectious disease of animals. The following amendment adds a provision that the Polish Hunting Union, tenants or managers of hunting circuits should provide information on the number of game animals present in the area and the estimated change in the number of these animals during the period concerned at the request of veterinary inspection bodies. The collection of these data by district and voivodship veterinarians will enable the planning of the sanitary discharge of wild game vectors of infectious diseases to be combated ex officio in order to minimise the spread of the disease. The draft law states that persons who thwart or impede the execution of activities resulting from the orders of veterinary inspection bodies issued in connection with the eradication of infectious animal diseases shall be fined, punishable by restrictions on liberty or imprisonment up to 3 years old. This will allow action to be carried out more effectively in the fight against infectious diseases by veterinary inspection bodies in the event of a lack of cooperation by animal owners or bystanders. The amount of fines has been determined where those prohibitions and orders are not enforced. Four amendments to the Act were proposed during the discussion of the draft law at the committee meeting. The Commission has examined the amendments tabled. Two of them were accepted. The entire bill was accepted without a vote of objection. The Commission recommends that the High House adopt it. It takes a moment at the front of the long-term struggle that we're fighting and we're going to have with African swine fever. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u435	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I have the honour to present my position on the committee's report on the Senate's resolution on the amendment of the Employment Promotion Act and the Labour Market Institutions Act and some other laws. The aim of the Act is to implement the so-called. Directive on seasonal workers. In its assumptions, among others. simplify procedures for the issue of work permits. However, the proposed changes in applying for a seasonal work permit for foreigners are not only much more complicated, but, moreover, do not take into account the particularities of agricultural production. Therefore, during the examination of the law by the Sejm on behalf of the Kukiz club15, I made seven amendments which met the expectations of farmers, gardeners and fruitists. Unfortunately, only one has been taken into account. The majority of the Sejm disagreed with the introduction of very important amendments, which may constitute further or not be the Polish fruit and vegetables sector. I regret to say that the Senate, which should be the House of reflection, did not carry out a thorough analysis of the effects of the proposed regulations and did not want to take over the proposed amendments. The new law provides for the issue of a seasonal work permit if the amount of remuneration specified in the contract with the foreigner is not lower than the remuneration of workers carrying out comparable work within the same period of time. Today the minimum rate is 13 PLN gross. The rate set at this level may lead to the abandonment of the collection of less cost-effective fruit and vegetable species and thus to a drastic deterioration in the financial condition of farms and the loss of the internal market and export markets. My proposal to exclude the application of this provision to seasonal work in agriculture has been rejected. Similarly, an amendment concerning the requirement to attach information to the application for authorisation concerning the foreseeable paid leave granted to the foreigner has happened. Members and senators do not realise that seasonal work in agriculture or gardening is short-term and unpredictable. Only the course of weather changes determines the breaks in work and during this period the seasonal worker is resting, and setting additional days off during a few weeks' period of employment for intensive work on the harvest will expose farmers to additional costs. I would like to make it clear that the adoption of the law as it stands will significantly reduce the competitiveness of Polish farms. This law is another attempt to improve the profitability of agricultural production of the old Union countries by increasing labour costs in the new Union countries, [[♪ The bell ♪]] i.e. also in Poland. I appeal to your Members for a moment of reflection and reflection on the vote. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u444	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the government proposal for a law amending the Act on Employment Promotion and Employment Market Institutions and some other laws. As I said at first reading, the amendments to the Act are related to the need to implement Directive 2014/36/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council. I have not changed my mind and will repeat: I have no doubt that the solutions currently in place in Poland do not provide workers and employers with adequate protection against abuse. The implementation of the directive in Polish legislation will result in a significant extension of the provisions of the law on the possibility of allowing foreigners to work on the Polish labour market. It is very good that the proposed amendment provides for a change in the registration system for job applications. This is where many abuses occur most often. In addition, the Directive introduces seasonal work permits. Importantly, all third-country nationals will be able to apply for this type of work. This is a significant extension of the current employment opportunities of foreigners on our market. Many provisions aim primarily at ordering the functioning of third-country workers in our legal system. Among the proposed provisions are a number of those which will serve to ensure the safety and decent working conditions of the incoming foreigners. These are records concerning e.g. the amount of the minimum wage or the need to guarantee an appropriate form of employment, similar to that adopted for domestic workers, social security. In my view, such provisions of the Act will have a positive impact on the reduction of discrimination against workers coming to us from other countries. However, what is extremely important, in the novel there are also a number of provisions that reduce such practices as making false statements or falsifying or buying statements. The procedure at the county labour offices, as well as at consulates, has so far been prolonged because of these situations. In practice, this has led to the fact that employers actually in need of an employee could not get it quickly. It is necessary to regulate the rules for the employment of foreigners in Poland more detailed than before. The legal influx of immigrants into Poland is also important because the resources of workers in our country are decreasing from year to year. The legal influx of immigrants is important for the smooth functioning of the economy as well as for the social development of the state. We should try to build such working conditions for foreigners so that they want to stay in our country and not just be seasonal workers. A very large group of seasonal workers are Ukrainians, but they only come to our country for a few months and then leave. I am aware that we are not able to compete, for example. with Germany in terms of wages, but we should create competitive conditions. I agree that the provisions introduced by this amendment will be an additional burden for employers wishing to make use of this possibility and that these solutions, due to the expansion of the administrative system and procedures, will also entail an increase in costs for the state budget. However, this is a small price incurred in exchange for the benefits of the bill submitted. The Free and Solidarni Circle supports the bill.

The Senate introduces the following amendment to the Act amending the Act on the Protection of Employee Claims in the event of the insolvency of the employer and the Act on Judicial Costs in Civil Matters: in Art. 1 in point 4 and in Article. 12a in the mouth. 7 in the first and third sentences, the words "submission of false statements' shall be replaced by the words "submission of false statements'. This provision provides that the Marshal of the voivodship shall pay the employees an advance against unmet claims by the employer, and the employee shall submit to the Marshal of the voivodship an application for payment of the advance and shall be required under criminal responsibility to include a statement containing certain information. The criminal liability clause reflects the regulation of the Article. 233 § 6 of the Penal Code, which constitutes criminal liability for lodging a false statement, not a statement. It's two different things to make false statements, and it's two different things to make a false statement. Since the Law refers several times to the appropriate application of the provision of the Article. 12a paragraph. 7 - in Article 7 (1) (a) (ii) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013. 15, Art. 16, Art. 23 - The Senate considered it appropriate to clarify in an amendment ensuring terminology consistency with the relevant provision of the Penal Code. And he did very well. To sum up, the Kukiz Poselski Club15 is applying for an amendment. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u472	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I'm not late anymore, I'm on time. I would like to present my position on behalf of the Kukiz Club15 on the Government's draft law on the ratification of the Headquarters Agreement between Rzeczpospolita Polska and the European Border and Coast Guard Agency (Frontex), signed in Warsaw on 9 March 2017. In the last few years, the priority of the European Union is to protect the borders of our continent. In the face of the influx of migrants from the countries of North Africa and the Middle East and the unstable situation in eastern Ukraine, we should seal our borders. There have been terrorist attacks in the West every month. Some of the bombers have reached Europe illegally, on the roads that have crossed our leaky European borders. The eastern land border of Poland is also one of the longest land sections of the external borders of the European Union. Poland runs the most important trans-European transport route east-west. Therefore, if we are talking about European solidarity, Poland should actively participate in the protection of the Union's borders. Because we are an important link in this protection, Frontex has chosen Warsaw as its headquarters. In view of the need to regulate the seat of the agency in our country, we should adopt this bill. With Frontex, Member States exposed to the influx of illegal immigrants can receive additional technical assistance, equipment and support for trained workers. The entry into force of the law will contribute to deepening cooperation between our country and Frontex. The Agency will plan, design and build its headquarters in Warsaw at its cost. According to estimates, it will allocate between EUR 100 and 120 million. Poland, for its part under the agreement, is to guarantee the reimbursement of the costs of education for the children of the Agency's employees, to plan a tax exemption for employees and to transfer the land to its premises free of charge. Kukiz15, recognising that the Republic of Poland should actively participate in the protection of the borders of the European Union, is in favour of deepening cooperation with the European Border and Coast Guard Agency. Thank you. [[Applause]]

It is understandable and natural that a Member State should ensure appropriate and appropriate conditions for agency staff and members of their families. Against the backdrop of a complicated international situation, from June 2016 onwards, there has been a strong acceleration in negotiating work aimed at developing a common view on the proposal to locate the future seat of the Agency in Poland. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u480	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I answer Mr Khrushch's question about security. That's the point. not only the Intelligence Agency, because a new headquarters of the Internal Security Agency is also envisaged in close proximity. But there's no security collision here. First of all, one thing must be emphasised: we are a member of the European Union and Frontex is also our agency. That's the way to deal with it. On the other hand, each of these agencies has its own means of securing which are adequate, adequate and modern, so that none of these institutions has raised objections to such a prospect of the neighbourhood, so there is no justification for this concern. As regards the status of staff, i.e. The participation of Poles in Frontex staff is currently at 1/3. It is true that we are not entirely satisfied with the rank of this staff. There must be a lot of different procedures to ensure that they are not just auxiliary, technical, or executive staff. The idea is that there should also be greater participation of Poles in the level of leadership of the agency. Of course, this is not the subject of this agreement. This agreement concerns the issue of seat, so the case requires additional action. On the other hand, with regard to Mr Sachajka's question of intent, on whose side this initiative came out here, well, the matter has a long history, because Frontex came to Poland in 2005, but unfortunately there has been no agreement for a very long time. The reasons were different, they could be analyzed for a long time. I can only say that the Government of Law and Justice has been making very intensive efforts since 2015 to make such an agreement signed. This was at a time of increasing tension caused by the migratory crisis, and there was also a very serious bitterness in the European Commission and in the Member States the lack of such an agreement for several years. It seems that the interests of other countries who wanted to take this opportunity to move this agency to each other were also known. They were talking about Italy, they were talking about Romania. This has put enough pressure on the government to negotiate, i.e. these negotiations have taken place in difficult or even very difficult conditions, and I have to say that the government has assumed that Frontex is precisely the agency which perfectly corresponds to the intentions and policies of the government, which has always placed the issue of border protection above all other problems related to migration policy and asylum policy. The difficulty and the high barrier that the European Commission put to the Polish Government was the adoption of the deadline for the final conclusion of this agreement, which was an exceptional solution, because it did not actually happen that a country based in such an agency would face such a condition. However, in a very short period of time these negotiations have managed to bring an end, which should be seen as the first very big success. The second very big success, which today was also emphasized in this Chamber, are the very favourable conditions under which this agreement has been concluded. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u482	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Poselski Club on the Government's draft law on ratification of the Headquarters Agreement between Rzeczpospolita Polska and the European Border and Coast Guard Agency (Frontex), signed in Warsaw on 9 March 2017, in prints no. 1694 and 1755. Ladies and gentlemen! This bill concerns the consent of the President of the Republic of Poland to ratify the Agreement on the seat between the Republic of Poland and the European Border and Coast Guard Agency (Frontex), signed in Warsaw on 9 March 2017. The draft agreement itself was drawn up in order to remedy this issue of the seat of Frontex in Poland and other matters relating to the functioning of the agency in our country. So far, the area governed by the agreement, namely the seat and other matters relating to the functioning of the Frontex agency in the territory of the Republic of Poland, has regulated the agreement between the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration and the Executive Director of the European Agency for the Management of Operational Cooperation at the External Borders of the Member States of the European Union of 26 March 2007. The agreement regulates issues, among others. the freedom of communication of the Agency, the identification cards of the Agency's staff, the location of its seat, the security and security of the Agency, the training of the children of officials and other staff of the Agency and the settlement of disputes. As a result of the amendment of Council Regulation No 2007/2004 of 26 October 2004 establishing a European Agency for the Management of Operational Cooperation at the External Borders of the Member States of the European Union (Frontex), which was the basis for the conclusion of the said agreement, a new agreement was necessary. As I said at the outset, the bill concerns the consent of the President of the Republic of Poland to ratify this new agreement. High Izbo! The entry into force of the new agreement will contribute to the deepening of cooperation between the Republic of Poland and Frontex and will provide the Agency with a permanent seat and conditions of operation in the territory of the Republic of Poland. I also agree with the proposal put forward by the government in the explanatory memorandum that the agreement shows that for the Polish side the protection of the external borders of the European Union is an extremely important element of the Polish government's policy. It is also another bond between Poland and the European Union, which will make it harder for the current majority to bring Poland out of the European Union. The Modern Posel Club is applying for the adoption by the High Chamber of the bill submitted. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-19-sejm-46-2.u484	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Public Finance, I have the honour to present a report in print No. 1758 on the Government's draft law amending the Law Tax Order, the Law on Public Finance and the Law introducing the Act on National Tax Administration, the original printing No. 1677. This project amends three laws: firstly, in the Tax Order, secondly, in the Public Finance Act and thirdly in the Rules implementing the National Tax Administration Act. As a result of the proposed amendments to the Act, the Director of National Tax Information will be obliged to ask the Head of National Tax Administration for an opinion only if the issue arising from the background of the facts or future event has not been previously assessed by the Head of National Tax Administration. The purpose of the proposed amendment provided for in Article. 1 point 2 of the draft law is to facilitate the implementation of the provisions of the Tax Order as regards the keeping of the Register of Tax Courts by supplementing the provision of the article. 43 Tax Order for a delegation enabling the keeping of a register other than the head of the National Tax Administration to be entrusted to a body of the National Tax Administration. The amendment contained in Article. 2 of the draft law, consisting of the addition of an article. 3a in the Public Finance Act of 27 August 2009 is linked to the need to explicitly exclude the application of Article. Article 18 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Act of 15 December 2016 on the Public Prosecutor's Office of the Republic of Poland in relation, first, to financial operations relating to the implementation of the State budget and the budget of European funds, including banking services, second, financial operations relating to the measures referred to in Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 points 2 and 3 of the Public Finance Act, and thirdly, borrowing and lending, issuing treasury securities and other operations directly related to the management of the State Treasury debt. At its meeting, the Committee on Public Finance adopted the amendment tabled to the article. 3 of the proposed law on Article. 259 Laws Introducing the National Tax Administration Act. This amendment aims to align the bill with the requirements of the Public Finance Act. Solutions contained in the said Article. 259 Laws Introducing the National Tax Administration Act are exceptional in nature and are justified in the case of the creation of new institutions during the financial year. In view of the fact that Article 2 of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 does not constitute State aid within the meaning of Article 107 (1) of the Treaty. The 9th National Tax Administration Act requires that the budget section dedicated to the National Tax Administration be separated, in the draft Budget Act for 2018 this part should already be separated. The amendment adopted maintains a temporary arrangement consisting in financing the activities of the National Tax Administration from the budget of the Ministry of Finance, but only until the end of this year. The Commission has also made legislative changes. The Committee on Public Finances requests the High Sejm to adopt the Commission's report, print No. 1758, government draft law amending the Tax Order, the Public Finance Act and the Law introducing the Act on National Tax Administration. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
